96/* This macro tells if we are in the context of loading an AOF. */
97#define isAOFLoadingContext() \
98    ((server.current_client && server.current_client->id == CLIENT_ID_AOF) ? 1 : 0)

100/* We use a private localtime implementation which is fork-safe. The logging
101 * function of Redis may be called from other threads. */
102void nolocks_localtime(struct tm *tmp, time_t t, time_t tz, int dst);
103
104/* Low level logging. To use only for very big messages, otherwise
105 * serverLog() is to prefer. */
106void serverLogRaw(int level, const char *msg) {
107    const int syslogLevelMap[] = { LOG_DEBUG, LOG_INFO, LOG_NOTICE, LOG_WARNING };
108    const char *c = ".-*#";
109    FILE *fp;
110    char buf[64];
111    int rawmode = (level & LL_RAW);
112    int log_to_stdout = server.logfile[0] == '\0';
113
114    level &= 0xff; /* clear flags */
115    if (level < server.verbosity) return;
116
117    fp = log_to_stdout ? stdout : fopen(server.logfile,"a");
118    if (!fp) return;
119
120    if (rawmode) {
121        fprintf(fp,"%s",msg);
122    } else {
123        int off;
124        struct timeval tv;
125        int role_char;
126        pid_t pid = getpid();
127
128        gettimeofday(&tv,NULL);
129        struct tm tm;
130        nolocks_localtime(&tm,tv.tv_sec,server.timezone,server.daylight_active);
131        off = strftime(buf,sizeof(buf),"%d %b %Y %H:%M:%S.",&tm);
132        snprintf(buf+off,sizeof(buf)-off,"%03d",(int)tv.tv_usec/1000);
133        if (server.sentinel_mode) {
134            role_char = 'X'; /* Sentinel. */
135        } else if (pid != server.pid) {
136            role_char = 'C'; /* RDB / AOF writing child. */
137        } else {
138            role_char = (server.masterhost ? 'S':'M'); /* Slave or Master. */
139        }
140        fprintf(fp,"%d:%c %s %c %s\n",
141            (int)getpid(),role_char, buf,c[level],msg);
142    }
143    fflush(fp);
144
145    if (!log_to_stdout) fclose(fp);
146    if (server.syslog_enabled) syslog(syslogLevelMap[level], "%s", msg);
147}
148
149/* Like serverLogRaw() but with printf-alike support. This is the function that
150 * is used across the code. The raw version is only used in order to dump
151 * the INFO output on crash. */
152void _serverLog(int level, const char *fmt, ...) {
153    va_list ap;
154    char msg[LOG_MAX_LEN];
155
156    va_start(ap, fmt);
157    vsnprintf(msg, sizeof(msg), fmt, ap);
158    va_end(ap);
159
160    serverLogRaw(level,msg);
161}
162
163/* Log a fixed message without printf-alike capabilities, in a way that is
164 * safe to call from a signal handler.
165 *
166 * We actually use this only for signals that are not fatal from the point
167 * of view of Redis. Signals that are going to kill the server anyway and
168 * where we need printf-alike features are served by serverLog(). */
169void serverLogFromHandler(int level, const char *msg) {
170    int fd;
171    int log_to_stdout = server.logfile[0] == '\0';
172    char buf[64];
173
174    if ((level&0xff) < server.verbosity || (log_to_stdout && server.daemonize))
175        return;
176    fd = log_to_stdout ? STDOUT_FILENO :
177                         open(server.logfile, O_APPEND|O_CREAT|O_WRONLY, 0644);
178    if (fd == -1) return;
179    ll2string(buf,sizeof(buf),getpid());
180    if (write(fd,buf,strlen(buf)) == -1) goto err;
181    if (write(fd,":signal-handler (",17) == -1) goto err;
182    ll2string(buf,sizeof(buf),time(NULL));
183    if (write(fd,buf,strlen(buf)) == -1) goto err;
184    if (write(fd,") ",2) == -1) goto err;
185    if (write(fd,msg,strlen(msg)) == -1) goto err;
186    if (write(fd,"\n",1) == -1) goto err;
187err:
188    if (!log_to_stdout) close(fd);
189}
190
191/* Return the UNIX time in microseconds */
192long long ustime(void) {
193    struct timeval tv;
194    long long ust;
195
196    gettimeofday(&tv, NULL);
197    ust = ((long long)tv.tv_sec)*1000000;
198    ust += tv.tv_usec;
199    return ust;
200}
201
202/* Return the UNIX time in milliseconds */
203mstime_t mstime(void) {
204    return ustime()/1000;
205}
206
207/* Return the command time snapshot in milliseconds.
208 * The time the command started is the logical time it runs,
209 * and all the time readings during the execution time should
210 * reflect the same time.
211 * More details can be found in the comments below. */
212mstime_t commandTimeSnapshot(void) {
213    /* When we are in the middle of a command execution, we want to use a
214     * reference time that does not change: in that case we just use the
215     * cached time, that we update before each call in the call() function.
216     * This way we avoid that commands such as RPOPLPUSH or similar, that
217     * may re-open the same key multiple times, can invalidate an already
218     * open object in a next call, if the next call will see the key expired,
219     * while the first did not.
220     * This is specifically important in the context of scripts, where we
221     * pretend that time freezes. This way a key can expire only the first time
222     * it is accessed and not in the middle of the script execution, making
223     * propagation to slaves / AOF consistent. See issue #1525 for more info.
224     * Note that we cannot use the cached server.mstime because it can change
225     * in processEventsWhileBlocked etc. */
226    return server.cmd_time_snapshot;
227}
228
229/* After an RDB dump or AOF rewrite we exit from children using _exit() instead of
230 * exit(), because the latter may interact with the same file objects used by
231 * the parent process. However if we are testing the coverage normal exit() is
232 * used in order to obtain the right coverage information. */
233void exitFromChild(int retcode) {
234#ifdef COVERAGE_TEST
235    exit(retcode);
236#else
237    _exit(retcode);
238#endif
239}

247void dictVanillaFree(dict *d, void *val)
248{
249    UNUSED(d);
250    zfree(val);
251}
252
253void dictListDestructor(dict *d, void *val)
254{
255    UNUSED(d);
256    listRelease((list*)val);
257}
258
259int dictSdsKeyCompare(dict *d, const void *key1,
260        const void *key2)
261{
262    int l1,l2;
263    UNUSED(d);
264
265    l1 = sdslen((sds)key1);
266    l2 = sdslen((sds)key2);
267    if (l1 != l2) return 0;
268    return memcmp(key1, key2, l1) == 0;
269}
270
271/* A case insensitive version used for the command lookup table and other
272 * places where case insensitive non binary-safe comparison is needed. */
273int dictSdsKeyCaseCompare(dict *d, const void *key1,
274        const void *key2)
275{
276    UNUSED(d);
277    return strcasecmp(key1, key2) == 0;
278}
279
280void dictObjectDestructor(dict *d, void *val)
281{
282    UNUSED(d);
283    if (val == NULL) return; /* Lazy freeing will set value to NULL. */
284    decrRefCount(val);
285}
286
287void dictSdsDestructor(dict *d, void *val)
288{
289    UNUSED(d);
290    sdsfree(val);
291}
292
293void *dictSdsDup(dict *d, const void *key) {
294    UNUSED(d);
295    return sdsdup((const sds) key);
296}
297
298int dictObjKeyCompare(dict *d, const void *key1,
299        const void *key2)
300{
301    const robj *o1 = key1, *o2 = key2;
302    return dictSdsKeyCompare(d, o1->ptr,o2->ptr);
303}

305uint64_t dictObjHash(const void *key) {
306    const robj *o = key;
307    return dictGenHashFunction(o->ptr, sdslen((sds)o->ptr));
308}
309
310uint64_t dictSdsHash(const void *key) {
311    return dictGenHashFunction((unsigned char*)key, sdslen((char*)key));
312}
313
314uint64_t dictSdsCaseHash(const void *key) {
315    return dictGenCaseHashFunction((unsigned char*)key, sdslen((char*)key));
316}

318/* Dict hash function for null terminated string */
319uint64_t dictCStrHash(const void *key) {
320    return dictGenHashFunction((unsigned char*)key, strlen((char*)key));
321}
322
323/* Dict hash function for null terminated string */
324uint64_t dictCStrCaseHash(const void *key) {
325    return dictGenCaseHashFunction((unsigned char*)key, strlen((char*)key));
326}

328/* Dict compare function for null terminated string */
329int dictCStrKeyCompare(dict *d, const void *key1, const void *key2) {
330    int l1,l2;
331    UNUSED(d);
332
333    l1 = strlen((char*)key1);
334    l2 = strlen((char*)key2);
335    if (l1 != l2) return 0;
336    return memcmp(key1, key2, l1) == 0;
337}
338
339/* Dict case insensitive compare function for null terminated string */
340int dictCStrKeyCaseCompare(dict *d, const void *key1, const void *key2) {
341    UNUSED(d);
342    return strcasecmp(key1, key2) == 0;
343}
344
345int dictEncObjKeyCompare(dict *d, const void *key1, const void *key2)
346{
347    robj *o1 = (robj*) key1, *o2 = (robj*) key2;
348    int cmp;
349
350    if (o1->encoding == OBJ_ENCODING_INT &&
351        o2->encoding == OBJ_ENCODING_INT)
352            return o1->ptr == o2->ptr;
353
354    /* Due to OBJ_STATIC_REFCOUNT, we avoid calling getDecodedObject() without
355     * good reasons, because it would incrRefCount() the object, which
356     * is invalid. So we check to make sure dictFind() works with static
357     * objects as well. */
358    if (o1->refcount != OBJ_STATIC_REFCOUNT) o1 = getDecodedObject(o1);
359    if (o2->refcount != OBJ_STATIC_REFCOUNT) o2 = getDecodedObject(o2);
360    cmp = dictSdsKeyCompare(d,o1->ptr,o2->ptr);
361    if (o1->refcount != OBJ_STATIC_REFCOUNT) decrRefCount(o1);
362    if (o2->refcount != OBJ_STATIC_REFCOUNT) decrRefCount(o2);
363    return cmp;
364}
365
366uint64_t dictEncObjHash(const void *key) {
367    robj *o = (robj*) key;
368
369    if (sdsEncodedObject(o)) {
370        return dictGenHashFunction(o->ptr, sdslen((sds)o->ptr));
371    } else if (o->encoding == OBJ_ENCODING_INT) {
372        char buf[32];
373        int len;
374
375        len = ll2string(buf,32,(long)o->ptr);
376        return dictGenHashFunction((unsigned char*)buf, len);
377    } else {
378        serverPanic("Unknown string encoding");
379    }
380}
381
382/* Return 1 if currently we allow dict to expand. Dict may allocate huge
383 * memory to contain hash buckets when dict expands, that may lead redis
384 * rejects user's requests or evicts some keys, we can stop dict to expand
385 * provisionally if used memory will be over maxmemory after dict expands,
386 * but to guarantee the performance of redis, we still allow dict to expand
387 * if dict load factor exceeds HASHTABLE_MAX_LOAD_FACTOR. */
388int dictExpandAllowed(size_t moreMem, double usedRatio) {
389    if (usedRatio <= HASHTABLE_MAX_LOAD_FACTOR) {
390        return !overMaxmemoryAfterAlloc(moreMem);
391    } else {
392        return 1;
393    }
394}

396/* Returns the size of the DB dict entry metadata in bytes. In cluster mode, the
397 * metadata is used for constructing a doubly linked list of the dict entries
398 * belonging to the same cluster slot. See the Slot to Key API in cluster.c. */
399size_t dbDictEntryMetadataSize(dict *d) {
400    UNUSED(d);
401    /* NOTICE: this also affects overhead_ht_slot_to_keys in getMemoryOverheadData.
402     * If we ever add non-cluster related data here, that code must be modified too. */
403    return server.cluster_enabled ? sizeof(clusterDictEntryMetadata) : 0;
404}
405
406/* Returns the size of the DB dict metadata in bytes. In cluster mode, we store
407 * a pointer to the db in the main db dict, used for updating the slot-to-key
408 * mapping when a dictEntry is reallocated. */
409size_t dbDictMetadataSize(void) {
410    return server.cluster_enabled ? sizeof(clusterDictMetadata) : 0;
411}
412
413void dbDictAfterReplaceEntry(dict *d, dictEntry *de) {
414    if (server.cluster_enabled) slotToKeyReplaceEntry(d, de);
415}

593int htNeedsResize(dict *dict) {
594    long long size, used;
595
596    size = dictSlots(dict);
597    used = dictSize(dict);
598    return (size > DICT_HT_INITIAL_SIZE &&
599            (used*100/size < HASHTABLE_MIN_FILL));
600}
601
602/* If the percentage of used slots in the HT reaches HASHTABLE_MIN_FILL
603 * we resize the hash table to save memory */
604void tryResizeHashTables(int dbid) {
605    if (htNeedsResize(server.db[dbid].dict))
606        dictResize(server.db[dbid].dict);
607    if (htNeedsResize(server.db[dbid].expires))
608        dictResize(server.db[dbid].expires);
609}
610
611/* Our hash table implementation performs rehashing incrementally while
612 * we write/read from the hash table. Still if the server is idle, the hash
613 * table will use two tables for a long time. So we try to use 1 millisecond
614 * of CPU time at every call of this function to perform some rehashing.
615 *
616 * The function returns 1 if some rehashing was performed, otherwise 0
617 * is returned. */
618int incrementallyRehash(int dbid) {
619    /* Keys dictionary */
620    if (dictIsRehashing(server.db[dbid].dict)) {
621        dictRehashMilliseconds(server.db[dbid].dict,1);
622        return 1; /* already used our millisecond for this loop... */
623    }
624    /* Expires */
625    if (dictIsRehashing(server.db[dbid].expires)) {
626        dictRehashMilliseconds(server.db[dbid].expires,1);
627        return 1; /* already used our millisecond for this loop... */
628    }
629    return 0;
630}
631
632/* This function is called once a background process of some kind terminates,
633 * as we want to avoid resizing the hash tables when there is a child in order
634 * to play well with copy-on-write (otherwise when a resize happens lots of
635 * memory pages are copied). The goal of this function is to update the ability
636 * for dict.c to resize or rehash the tables accordingly to the fact we have an
637 * active fork child running. */
638void updateDictResizePolicy(void) {
639    if (server.in_fork_child != CHILD_TYPE_NONE)
640        dictSetResizeEnabled(DICT_RESIZE_FORBID);
641    else if (hasActiveChildProcess())
642        dictSetResizeEnabled(DICT_RESIZE_AVOID);
643    else
644        dictSetResizeEnabled(DICT_RESIZE_ENABLE);
645}
646
647const char *strChildType(int type) {
648    switch(type) {
649        case CHILD_TYPE_RDB: return "RDB";
650        case CHILD_TYPE_AOF: return "AOF";
651        case CHILD_TYPE_LDB: return "LDB";
652        case CHILD_TYPE_MODULE: return "MODULE";
653        default: return "Unknown";
654    }
655}
656
657/* Return true if there are active children processes doing RDB saving,
658 * AOF rewriting, or some side process spawned by a loaded module. */
659int hasActiveChildProcess(void) {
660    return server.child_pid != -1;
661}
662
663void resetChildState(void) {
664    server.child_type = CHILD_TYPE_NONE;
665    server.child_pid = -1;
666    server.stat_current_cow_peak = 0;
667    server.stat_current_cow_bytes = 0;
668    server.stat_current_cow_updated = 0;
669    server.stat_current_save_keys_processed = 0;
670    server.stat_module_progress = 0;
671    server.stat_current_save_keys_total = 0;
672    updateDictResizePolicy();
673    closeChildInfoPipe();
674    moduleFireServerEvent(REDISMODULE_EVENT_FORK_CHILD,
675                          REDISMODULE_SUBEVENT_FORK_CHILD_DIED,
676                          NULL);
677}
678
679/* Return if child type is mutually exclusive with other fork children */
680int isMutuallyExclusiveChildType(int type) {
681    return type == CHILD_TYPE_RDB || type == CHILD_TYPE_AOF || type == CHILD_TYPE_MODULE;
682}
683
684/* Returns true when we're inside a long command that yielded to the event loop. */
685int isInsideYieldingLongCommand(void) {
686    return scriptIsTimedout() || server.busy_module_yield_flags;
687}
688
689/* Return true if this instance has persistence completely turned off:
690 * both RDB and AOF are disabled. */
691int allPersistenceDisabled(void) {
692    return server.saveparamslen == 0 && server.aof_state == AOF_OFF;
693}
694
695/* ======================= Cron: called every 100 ms ======================== */
696
697/* Add a sample to the instantaneous metric. This function computes the quotient
698 * of the increment of value and base, which is useful to record operation count
699 * per second, or the average time consumption of an operation.
700 * 
701 * current_value - The dividend
702 * current_base - The divisor
703 * */
704void trackInstantaneousMetric(int metric, long long current_value, long long current_base, long long factor) {
705    if (server.inst_metric[metric].last_sample_base > 0) {
706        long long base = current_base - server.inst_metric[metric].last_sample_base;
707        long long value = current_value - server.inst_metric[metric].last_sample_value;
708        long long avg = base > 0 ? (value * factor / base) : 0;
709        server.inst_metric[metric].samples[server.inst_metric[metric].idx] = avg;
710        server.inst_metric[metric].idx++;
711        server.inst_metric[metric].idx %= STATS_METRIC_SAMPLES;
712    }
713    server.inst_metric[metric].last_sample_base = current_base;
714    server.inst_metric[metric].last_sample_value = current_value;
715}
716
717/* Return the mean of all the samples. */
718long long getInstantaneousMetric(int metric) {
719    int j;
720    long long sum = 0;
721
722    for (j = 0; j < STATS_METRIC_SAMPLES; j++)
723        sum += server.inst_metric[metric].samples[j];
724    return sum / STATS_METRIC_SAMPLES;
725}
726
727/* The client query buffer is an sds.c string that can end with a lot of
728 * free space not used, this function reclaims space if needed.
729 *
730 * The function always returns 0 as it never terminates the client. */
731int clientsCronResizeQueryBuffer(client *c) {
732    size_t querybuf_size = sdsalloc(c->querybuf);
733    time_t idletime = server.unixtime - c->lastinteraction;
734
735    /* Only resize the query buffer if the buffer is actually wasting at least a
736     * few kbytes */
737    if (sdsavail(c->querybuf) > 1024*4) {
738        /* There are two conditions to resize the query buffer: */
739        if (idletime > 2) {
740            /* 1) Query is idle for a long time. */
741            c->querybuf = sdsRemoveFreeSpace(c->querybuf, 1);
742        } else if (querybuf_size > PROTO_RESIZE_THRESHOLD && querybuf_size/2 > c->querybuf_peak) {
743            /* 2) Query buffer is too big for latest peak and is larger than
744             *    resize threshold. Trim excess space but only up to a limit,
745             *    not below the recent peak and current c->querybuf (which will
746             *    be soon get used). If we're in the middle of a bulk then make
747             *    sure not to resize to less than the bulk length. */
748            size_t resize = sdslen(c->querybuf);
749            if (resize < c->querybuf_peak) resize = c->querybuf_peak;
750            if (c->bulklen != -1 && resize < (size_t)c->bulklen + 2) resize = c->bulklen + 2;
751            c->querybuf = sdsResize(c->querybuf, resize, 1);
752        }
753    }
754
755    /* Reset the peak again to capture the peak memory usage in the next
756     * cycle. */
757    c->querybuf_peak = sdslen(c->querybuf);
758    /* We reset to either the current used, or currently processed bulk size,
759     * which ever is bigger. */
760    if (c->bulklen != -1 && (size_t)c->bulklen + 2 > c->querybuf_peak) c->querybuf_peak = c->bulklen + 2;
761    return 0;
762}
763
764/* The client output buffer can be adjusted to better fit the memory requirements.
765 *
766 * the logic is:
767 * in case the last observed peak size of the buffer equals the buffer size - we double the size
768 * in case the last observed peak size of the buffer is less than half the buffer size - we shrink by half.
769 * The buffer peak will be reset back to the buffer position every server.reply_buffer_peak_reset_time milliseconds
770 * The function always returns 0 as it never terminates the client. */
771int clientsCronResizeOutputBuffer(client *c, mstime_t now_ms) {
772
773    size_t new_buffer_size = 0;
774    char *oldbuf = NULL;
775    const size_t buffer_target_shrink_size = c->buf_usable_size/2;
776    const size_t buffer_target_expand_size = c->buf_usable_size*2;
777
778    /* in case the resizing is disabled return immediately */
779    if(!server.reply_buffer_resizing_enabled)
780        return 0;
781
782    if (buffer_target_shrink_size >= PROTO_REPLY_MIN_BYTES &&
783        c->buf_peak < buffer_target_shrink_size )
784    {
785        new_buffer_size = max(PROTO_REPLY_MIN_BYTES,c->buf_peak+1);
786        server.stat_reply_buffer_shrinks++;
787    } else if (buffer_target_expand_size < PROTO_REPLY_CHUNK_BYTES*2 &&
788        c->buf_peak == c->buf_usable_size)
789    {
790        new_buffer_size = min(PROTO_REPLY_CHUNK_BYTES,buffer_target_expand_size);
791        server.stat_reply_buffer_expands++;
792    }
793
794    serverAssertWithInfo(c, NULL, (!new_buffer_size) || (new_buffer_size >= (size_t)c->bufpos));
795
796    /* reset the peak value each server.reply_buffer_peak_reset_time seconds. in case the client will be idle
797     * it will start to shrink.
798     */
799    if (server.reply_buffer_peak_reset_time >=0 &&
800        now_ms - c->buf_peak_last_reset_time >= server.reply_buffer_peak_reset_time)
801    {
802        c->buf_peak = c->bufpos;
803        c->buf_peak_last_reset_time = now_ms;
804    }
805
806    if (new_buffer_size) {
807        oldbuf = c->buf;
808        c->buf = zmalloc_usable(new_buffer_size, &c->buf_usable_size);
809        memcpy(c->buf,oldbuf,c->bufpos);
810        zfree(oldbuf);
811    }
812    return 0;
813}
814
815/* This function is used in order to track clients using the biggest amount
816 * of memory in the latest few seconds. This way we can provide such information
817 * in the INFO output (clients section), without having to do an O(N) scan for
818 * all the clients.
819 *
820 * This is how it works. We have an array of CLIENTS_PEAK_MEM_USAGE_SLOTS slots
821 * where we track, for each, the biggest client output and input buffers we
822 * saw in that slot. Every slot corresponds to one of the latest seconds, since
823 * the array is indexed by doing UNIXTIME % CLIENTS_PEAK_MEM_USAGE_SLOTS.
824 *
825 * When we want to know what was recently the peak memory usage, we just scan
826 * such few slots searching for the maximum value. */
827#define CLIENTS_PEAK_MEM_USAGE_SLOTS 8
828size_t ClientsPeakMemInput[CLIENTS_PEAK_MEM_USAGE_SLOTS] = {0};
829size_t ClientsPeakMemOutput[CLIENTS_PEAK_MEM_USAGE_SLOTS] = {0};
830
831int clientsCronTrackExpansiveClients(client *c, int time_idx) {
832    size_t in_usage = sdsZmallocSize(c->querybuf) + c->argv_len_sum +
833	              (c->argv ? zmalloc_size(c->argv) : 0);
834    size_t out_usage = getClientOutputBufferMemoryUsage(c);
835
836    /* Track the biggest values observed so far in this slot. */
837    if (in_usage > ClientsPeakMemInput[time_idx]) ClientsPeakMemInput[time_idx] = in_usage;
838    if (out_usage > ClientsPeakMemOutput[time_idx]) ClientsPeakMemOutput[time_idx] = out_usage;
839
840    return 0; /* This function never terminates the client. */
841}
842
843/* All normal clients are placed in one of the "mem usage buckets" according
844 * to how much memory they currently use. We use this function to find the
845 * appropriate bucket based on a given memory usage value. The algorithm simply
846 * does a log2(mem) to ge the bucket. This means, for examples, that if a
847 * client's memory usage doubles it's moved up to the next bucket, if it's
848 * halved we move it down a bucket.
849 * For more details see CLIENT_MEM_USAGE_BUCKETS documentation in server.h. */
850static inline clientMemUsageBucket *getMemUsageBucket(size_t mem) {
851    int size_in_bits = 8*(int)sizeof(mem);
852    int clz = mem > 0 ? __builtin_clzl(mem) : size_in_bits;
853    int bucket_idx = size_in_bits - clz;
854    if (bucket_idx > CLIENT_MEM_USAGE_BUCKET_MAX_LOG)
855        bucket_idx = CLIENT_MEM_USAGE_BUCKET_MAX_LOG;
856    else if (bucket_idx < CLIENT_MEM_USAGE_BUCKET_MIN_LOG)
857        bucket_idx = CLIENT_MEM_USAGE_BUCKET_MIN_LOG;
858    bucket_idx -= CLIENT_MEM_USAGE_BUCKET_MIN_LOG;
859    return &server.client_mem_usage_buckets[bucket_idx];
860}
861
862/*
863 * This method updates the client memory usage and update the
864 * server stats for client type.
865 *
866 * This method is called from the clientsCron to have updated
867 * stats for non CLIENT_TYPE_NORMAL/PUBSUB clients to accurately
868 * provide information around clients memory usage.
869 *
870 * It is also used in updateClientMemUsageAndBucket to have latest
871 * client memory usage information to place it into appropriate client memory
872 * usage bucket.
873 */
874void updateClientMemoryUsage(client *c) {
875    size_t mem = getClientMemoryUsage(c, NULL);
876    int type = getClientType(c);
877    /* Now that we have the memory used by the client, remove the old
878     * value from the old category, and add it back. */
879    server.stat_clients_type_memory[c->last_memory_type] -= c->last_memory_usage;
880    server.stat_clients_type_memory[type] += mem;
881    /* Remember what we added and where, to remove it next time. */
882    c->last_memory_type = type;
883    c->last_memory_usage = mem;
884}
885
886int clientEvictionAllowed(client *c) {
887    if (server.maxmemory_clients == 0 || c->flags & CLIENT_NO_EVICT) {
888        return 0;
889    }
890    int type = getClientType(c);
891    return (type == CLIENT_TYPE_NORMAL || type == CLIENT_TYPE_PUBSUB);
892}
893
894
895/* This function is used to cleanup the client's previously tracked memory usage.
896 * This is called during incremental client memory usage tracking as well as
897 * used to reset when client to bucket allocation is not required when 
898 * client eviction is disabled.  */
899void removeClientFromMemUsageBucket(client *c, int allow_eviction) {
900    if (c->mem_usage_bucket) {
901        c->mem_usage_bucket->mem_usage_sum -= c->last_memory_usage;
902        /* If this client can't be evicted then remove it from the mem usage
903         * buckets */
904        if (!allow_eviction) {
905            listDelNode(c->mem_usage_bucket->clients, c->mem_usage_bucket_node);
906            c->mem_usage_bucket = NULL;
907            c->mem_usage_bucket_node = NULL;
908        }
909    }
910}
911
912/* This is called only if explicit clients when something changed their buffers,
913 * so we can track clients' memory and enforce clients' maxmemory in real time.
914 *
915 * This also adds the client to the correct memory usage bucket. Each bucket contains
916 * all clients with roughly the same amount of memory. This way we group
917 * together clients consuming about the same amount of memory and can quickly
918 * free them in case we reach maxmemory-clients (client eviction).
919 *
920 * Note: This function filters clients of type no-evict, master or replica regardless
921 * of whether the eviction is enabled or not, so the memory usage we get from these
922 * types of clients via the INFO command may be out of date.
923 *
924 * returns 1 if client eviction for this client is allowed, 0 otherwise.
925 */
926int updateClientMemUsageAndBucket(client *c) {
927    serverAssert(io_threads_op == IO_THREADS_OP_IDLE);
928    int allow_eviction = clientEvictionAllowed(c);
929    removeClientFromMemUsageBucket(c, allow_eviction);
930
931    if (!allow_eviction) {
932        return 0;
933    }
934
935    /* Update client memory usage. */
936    updateClientMemoryUsage(c);
937
938    /* Update the client in the mem usage buckets */
939    clientMemUsageBucket *bucket = getMemUsageBucket(c->last_memory_usage);
940    bucket->mem_usage_sum += c->last_memory_usage;
941    if (bucket != c->mem_usage_bucket) {
942        if (c->mem_usage_bucket)
943            listDelNode(c->mem_usage_bucket->clients,
944                        c->mem_usage_bucket_node);
945        c->mem_usage_bucket = bucket;
946        listAddNodeTail(bucket->clients, c);
947        c->mem_usage_bucket_node = listLast(bucket->clients);
948    }
949    return 1;
950}
951
952/* Return the max samples in the memory usage of clients tracked by
953 * the function clientsCronTrackExpansiveClients(). */
954void getExpansiveClientsInfo(size_t *in_usage, size_t *out_usage) {
955    size_t i = 0, o = 0;
956    for (int j = 0; j < CLIENTS_PEAK_MEM_USAGE_SLOTS; j++) {
957        if (ClientsPeakMemInput[j] > i) i = ClientsPeakMemInput[j];
958        if (ClientsPeakMemOutput[j] > o) o = ClientsPeakMemOutput[j];
959    }
960    *in_usage = i;
961    *out_usage = o;
962}
963
964/* This function is called by serverCron() and is used in order to perform
965 * operations on clients that are important to perform constantly. For instance
966 * we use this function in order to disconnect clients after a timeout, including
967 * clients blocked in some blocking command with a non-zero timeout.
968 *
969 * The function makes some effort to process all the clients every second, even
970 * if this cannot be strictly guaranteed, since serverCron() may be called with
971 * an actual frequency lower than server.hz in case of latency events like slow
972 * commands.
973 *
974 * It is very important for this function, and the functions it calls, to be
975 * very fast: sometimes Redis has tens of hundreds of connected clients, and the
976 * default server.hz value is 10, so sometimes here we need to process thousands
977 * of clients per second, turning this function into a source of latency.
978 */
979#define CLIENTS_CRON_MIN_ITERATIONS 5
980void clientsCron(void) {
981    /* Try to process at least numclients/server.hz of clients
982     * per call. Since normally (if there are no big latency events) this
983     * function is called server.hz times per second, in the average case we
984     * process all the clients in 1 second. */
985    int numclients = listLength(server.clients);
986    int iterations = numclients/server.hz;
987    mstime_t now = mstime();
988
989    /* Process at least a few clients while we are at it, even if we need
990     * to process less than CLIENTS_CRON_MIN_ITERATIONS to meet our contract
991     * of processing each client once per second. */
992    if (iterations < CLIENTS_CRON_MIN_ITERATIONS)
993        iterations = (numclients < CLIENTS_CRON_MIN_ITERATIONS) ?
994                     numclients : CLIENTS_CRON_MIN_ITERATIONS;
995
996
997    int curr_peak_mem_usage_slot = server.unixtime % CLIENTS_PEAK_MEM_USAGE_SLOTS;
998    /* Always zero the next sample, so that when we switch to that second, we'll
999     * only register samples that are greater in that second without considering
1000     * the history of such slot.
1001     *
1002     * Note: our index may jump to any random position if serverCron() is not
1003     * called for some reason with the normal frequency, for instance because
1004     * some slow command is called taking multiple seconds to execute. In that
1005     * case our array may end containing data which is potentially older
1006     * than CLIENTS_PEAK_MEM_USAGE_SLOTS seconds: however this is not a problem
1007     * since here we want just to track if "recently" there were very expansive
1008     * clients from the POV of memory usage. */
1009    int zeroidx = (curr_peak_mem_usage_slot+1) % CLIENTS_PEAK_MEM_USAGE_SLOTS;
1010    ClientsPeakMemInput[zeroidx] = 0;
1011    ClientsPeakMemOutput[zeroidx] = 0;
1012
1013
1014    while(listLength(server.clients) && iterations--) {
1015        client *c;
1016        listNode *head;
1017
1018        /* Take the current head, process, and then rotate the head to tail.
1019         * This way we can fairly iterate all clients step by step. */
1020        head = listFirst(server.clients);
1021        c = listNodeValue(head);
1022        listRotateHeadToTail(server.clients);
1023        /* The following functions do different service checks on the client.
1024         * The protocol is that they return non-zero if the client was
1025         * terminated. */
1026        if (clientsCronHandleTimeout(c,now)) continue;
1027        if (clientsCronResizeQueryBuffer(c)) continue;
1028        if (clientsCronResizeOutputBuffer(c,now)) continue;
1029
1030        if (clientsCronTrackExpansiveClients(c, curr_peak_mem_usage_slot)) continue;
1031
1032        /* Iterating all the clients in getMemoryOverheadData() is too slow and
1033         * in turn would make the INFO command too slow. So we perform this
1034         * computation incrementally and track the (not instantaneous but updated
1035         * to the second) total memory used by clients using clientsCron() in
1036         * a more incremental way (depending on server.hz).
1037         * If client eviction is enabled, update the bucket as well. */
1038        if (!updateClientMemUsageAndBucket(c))
1039            updateClientMemoryUsage(c);
1040
1041        if (closeClientOnOutputBufferLimitReached(c, 0)) continue;
1042    }
1043}
1044
1045/* This function handles 'background' operations we are required to do
1046 * incrementally in Redis databases, such as active key expiring, resizing,
1047 * rehashing. */
1048void databasesCron(void) {
1049    /* Expire keys by random sampling. Not required for slaves
1050     * as master will synthesize DELs for us. */
1051    if (server.active_expire_enabled) {
1052        if (iAmMaster()) {
1053            activeExpireCycle(ACTIVE_EXPIRE_CYCLE_SLOW);
1054        } else {
1055            expireSlaveKeys();
1056        }
1057    }
1058
1059    /* Defrag keys gradually. */
1060    activeDefragCycle();
1061
1062    /* Perform hash tables rehashing if needed, but only if there are no
1063     * other processes saving the DB on disk. Otherwise rehashing is bad
1064     * as will cause a lot of copy-on-write of memory pages. */
1065    if (!hasActiveChildProcess()) {
1066        /* We use global counters so if we stop the computation at a given
1067         * DB we'll be able to start from the successive in the next
1068         * cron loop iteration. */
1069        static unsigned int resize_db = 0;
1070        static unsigned int rehash_db = 0;
1071        int dbs_per_call = CRON_DBS_PER_CALL;
1072        int j;
1073
1074        /* Don't test more DBs than we have. */
1075        if (dbs_per_call > server.dbnum) dbs_per_call = server.dbnum;
1076
1077        /* Resize */
1078        for (j = 0; j < dbs_per_call; j++) {
1079            tryResizeHashTables(resize_db % server.dbnum);
1080            resize_db++;
1081        }
1082
1083        /* Rehash */
1084        if (server.activerehashing) {
1085            for (j = 0; j < dbs_per_call; j++) {
1086                int work_done = incrementallyRehash(rehash_db);
1087                if (work_done) {
1088                    /* If the function did some work, stop here, we'll do
1089                     * more at the next cron loop. */
1090                    break;
1091                } else {
1092                    /* If this db didn't need rehash, we'll try the next one. */
1093                    rehash_db++;
1094                    rehash_db %= server.dbnum;
1095                }
1096            }
1097        }
1098    }
1099}
1100
1101static inline void updateCachedTimeWithUs(int update_daylight_info, const long long ustime) {
1102    server.ustime = ustime;
1103    server.mstime = server.ustime / 1000;
1104    time_t unixtime = server.mstime / 1000;
1105    atomicSet(server.unixtime, unixtime);
1106
1107    /* To get information about daylight saving time, we need to call
1108     * localtime_r and cache the result. However calling localtime_r in this
1109     * context is safe since we will never fork() while here, in the main
1110     * thread. The logging function will call a thread safe version of
1111     * localtime that has no locks. */
1112    if (update_daylight_info) {
1113        struct tm tm;
1114        time_t ut = server.unixtime;
1115        localtime_r(&ut,&tm);
1116        server.daylight_active = tm.tm_isdst;
1117    }
1118}
1119
1120/* We take a cached value of the unix time in the global state because with
1121 * virtual memory and aging there is to store the current time in objects at
1122 * every object access, and accuracy is not needed. To access a global var is
1123 * a lot faster than calling time(NULL).
1124 *
1125 * This function should be fast because it is called at every command execution
1126 * in call(), so it is possible to decide if to update the daylight saving
1127 * info or not using the 'update_daylight_info' argument. Normally we update
1128 * such info only when calling this function from serverCron() but not when
1129 * calling it from call(). */
1130void updateCachedTime(int update_daylight_info) {
1131    const long long us = ustime();
1132    updateCachedTimeWithUs(update_daylight_info, us);
1133}
1134
1135/* Performing required operations in order to enter an execution unit.
1136 * In general, if we are already inside an execution unit then there is nothing to do,
1137 * otherwise we need to update cache times so the same cached time will be used all over
1138 * the execution unit.
1139 * update_cached_time - if 0, will not update the cached time even if required.
1140 * us - if not zero, use this time for cached time, otherwise get current time. */
1141void enterExecutionUnit(int update_cached_time, long long us) {
1142    if (server.execution_nesting++ == 0 && update_cached_time) {
1143        if (us == 0) {
1144            us = ustime();
1145        }
1146        updateCachedTimeWithUs(0, us);
1147        server.cmd_time_snapshot = server.mstime;
1148    }
1149}
1150
1151void exitExecutionUnit(void) {
1152    --server.execution_nesting;
1153}
1154
1155void checkChildrenDone(void) {
1156    int statloc = 0;
1157    pid_t pid;
1158
1159    if ((pid = waitpid(-1, &statloc, WNOHANG)) != 0) {
1160        int exitcode = WIFEXITED(statloc) ? WEXITSTATUS(statloc) : -1;
1161        int bysignal = 0;
1162
1163        if (WIFSIGNALED(statloc)) bysignal = WTERMSIG(statloc);
1164
1165        /* sigKillChildHandler catches the signal and calls exit(), but we
1166         * must make sure not to flag lastbgsave_status, etc incorrectly.
1167         * We could directly terminate the child process via SIGUSR1
1168         * without handling it */
1169        if (exitcode == SERVER_CHILD_NOERROR_RETVAL) {
1170            bysignal = SIGUSR1;
1171            exitcode = 1;
1172        }
1173
1174        if (pid == -1) {
1175            serverLog(LL_WARNING,"waitpid() returned an error: %s. "
1176                "child_type: %s, child_pid = %d",
1177                strerror(errno),
1178                strChildType(server.child_type),
1179                (int) server.child_pid);
1180        } else if (pid == server.child_pid) {
1181            if (server.child_type == CHILD_TYPE_RDB) {
1182                backgroundSaveDoneHandler(exitcode, bysignal);
1183            } else if (server.child_type == CHILD_TYPE_AOF) {
1184                backgroundRewriteDoneHandler(exitcode, bysignal);
1185            } else if (server.child_type == CHILD_TYPE_MODULE) {
1186                ModuleForkDoneHandler(exitcode, bysignal);
1187            } else {
1188                serverPanic("Unknown child type %d for child pid %d", server.child_type, server.child_pid);
1189                exit(1);
1190            }
1191            if (!bysignal && exitcode == 0) receiveChildInfo();
1192            resetChildState();
1193        } else {
1194            if (!ldbRemoveChild(pid)) {
1195                serverLog(LL_WARNING,
1196                          "Warning, detected child with unmatched pid: %ld",
1197                          (long) pid);
1198            }
1199        }
1200
1201        /* start any pending forks immediately. */
1202        replicationStartPendingFork();
1203    }
1204}
1205
1206/* Called from serverCron and cronUpdateMemoryStats to update cached memory metrics. */
1207void cronUpdateMemoryStats(void) {
1208    /* Record the max memory used since the server was started. */
1209    if (zmalloc_used_memory() > server.stat_peak_memory)
1210        server.stat_peak_memory = zmalloc_used_memory();
1211
1212    run_with_period(100) {
1213        /* Sample the RSS and other metrics here since this is a relatively slow call.
1214         * We must sample the zmalloc_used at the same time we take the rss, otherwise
1215         * the frag ratio calculate may be off (ratio of two samples at different times) */
1216        server.cron_malloc_stats.process_rss = zmalloc_get_rss();
1217        server.cron_malloc_stats.zmalloc_used = zmalloc_used_memory();
1218        /* Sampling the allocator info can be slow too.
1219         * The fragmentation ratio it'll show is potentially more accurate
1220         * it excludes other RSS pages such as: shared libraries, LUA and other non-zmalloc
1221         * allocations, and allocator reserved pages that can be pursed (all not actual frag) */
1222        zmalloc_get_allocator_info(&server.cron_malloc_stats.allocator_allocated,
1223                                   &server.cron_malloc_stats.allocator_active,
1224                                   &server.cron_malloc_stats.allocator_resident);
1225        /* in case the allocator isn't providing these stats, fake them so that
1226         * fragmentation info still shows some (inaccurate metrics) */
1227        if (!server.cron_malloc_stats.allocator_resident) {
1228            /* LUA memory isn't part of zmalloc_used, but it is part of the process RSS,
1229             * so we must deduct it in order to be able to calculate correct
1230             * "allocator fragmentation" ratio */
1231            size_t lua_memory = evalMemory();
1232            server.cron_malloc_stats.allocator_resident = server.cron_malloc_stats.process_rss - lua_memory;
1233        }
1234        if (!server.cron_malloc_stats.allocator_active)
1235            server.cron_malloc_stats.allocator_active = server.cron_malloc_stats.allocator_resident;
1236        if (!server.cron_malloc_stats.allocator_allocated)
1237            server.cron_malloc_stats.allocator_allocated = server.cron_malloc_stats.zmalloc_used;
1238    }
1239}
1240
1241/* This is our timer interrupt, called server.hz times per second.
1242 * Here is where we do a number of things that need to be done asynchronously.
1243 * For instance:
1244 *
1245 * - Active expired keys collection (it is also performed in a lazy way on
1246 *   lookup).
1247 * - Software watchdog.
1248 * - Update some statistic.
1249 * - Incremental rehashing of the DBs hash tables.
1250 * - Triggering BGSAVE / AOF rewrite, and handling of terminated children.
1251 * - Clients timeout of different kinds.
1252 * - Replication reconnection.
1253 * - Many more...
1254 *
1255 * Everything directly called here will be called server.hz times per second,
1256 * so in order to throttle execution of things we want to do less frequently
1257 * a macro is used: run_with_period(milliseconds) { .... }
1258 */
1259
1260int serverCron(struct aeEventLoop *eventLoop, long long id, void *clientData) {
1261    int j;
1262    UNUSED(eventLoop);
1263    UNUSED(id);
1264    UNUSED(clientData);
1265
1266    /* Software watchdog: deliver the SIGALRM that will reach the signal
1267     * handler if we don't return here fast enough. */
1268    if (server.watchdog_period) watchdogScheduleSignal(server.watchdog_period);
1269
1270    server.hz = server.config_hz;
1271    /* Adapt the server.hz value to the number of configured clients. If we have
1272     * many clients, we want to call serverCron() with an higher frequency. */
1273    if (server.dynamic_hz) {
1274        while (listLength(server.clients) / server.hz >
1275               MAX_CLIENTS_PER_CLOCK_TICK)
1276        {
1277            server.hz *= 2;
1278            if (server.hz > CONFIG_MAX_HZ) {
1279                server.hz = CONFIG_MAX_HZ;
1280                break;
1281            }
1282        }
1283    }
1284
1285    /* for debug purposes: skip actual cron work if pause_cron is on */
1286    if (server.pause_cron) return 1000/server.hz;
1287
1288    monotime cron_start = getMonotonicUs();
1289
1290    run_with_period(100) {
1291        long long stat_net_input_bytes, stat_net_output_bytes;
1292        long long stat_net_repl_input_bytes, stat_net_repl_output_bytes;
1293        atomicGet(server.stat_net_input_bytes, stat_net_input_bytes);
1294        atomicGet(server.stat_net_output_bytes, stat_net_output_bytes);
1295        atomicGet(server.stat_net_repl_input_bytes, stat_net_repl_input_bytes);
1296        atomicGet(server.stat_net_repl_output_bytes, stat_net_repl_output_bytes);
1297        monotime current_time = getMonotonicUs();
1298        long long factor = 1000000;  // us
1299        trackInstantaneousMetric(STATS_METRIC_COMMAND, server.stat_numcommands, current_time, factor);
1300        trackInstantaneousMetric(STATS_METRIC_NET_INPUT, stat_net_input_bytes + stat_net_repl_input_bytes,
1301                                 current_time, factor);
1302        trackInstantaneousMetric(STATS_METRIC_NET_OUTPUT, stat_net_output_bytes + stat_net_repl_output_bytes,
1303                                 current_time, factor);
1304        trackInstantaneousMetric(STATS_METRIC_NET_INPUT_REPLICATION, stat_net_repl_input_bytes, current_time,
1305                                 factor);
1306        trackInstantaneousMetric(STATS_METRIC_NET_OUTPUT_REPLICATION, stat_net_repl_output_bytes,
1307                                 current_time, factor);
1308        trackInstantaneousMetric(STATS_METRIC_EL_CYCLE, server.duration_stats[EL_DURATION_TYPE_EL].cnt,
1309                                 current_time, factor);
1310        trackInstantaneousMetric(STATS_METRIC_EL_DURATION, server.duration_stats[EL_DURATION_TYPE_EL].sum,
1311                                 server.duration_stats[EL_DURATION_TYPE_EL].cnt, 1);
1312    }
1313
1314    /* We have just LRU_BITS bits per object for LRU information.
1315     * So we use an (eventually wrapping) LRU clock.
1316     *
1317     * Note that even if the counter wraps it's not a big problem,
1318     * everything will still work but some object will appear younger
1319     * to Redis. However for this to happen a given object should never be
1320     * touched for all the time needed to the counter to wrap, which is
1321     * not likely.
1322     *
1323     * Note that you can change the resolution altering the
1324     * LRU_CLOCK_RESOLUTION define. */
1325    server.lruclock = getLRUClock();
1326
1327    cronUpdateMemoryStats();
1328
1329    /* We received a SIGTERM or SIGINT, shutting down here in a safe way, as it is
1330     * not ok doing so inside the signal handler. */
1331    if (server.shutdown_asap && !isShutdownInitiated()) {
1332        int shutdownFlags = SHUTDOWN_NOFLAGS;
1333        if (server.last_sig_received == SIGINT && server.shutdown_on_sigint)
1334            shutdownFlags = server.shutdown_on_sigint;
1335        else if (server.last_sig_received == SIGTERM && server.shutdown_on_sigterm)
1336            shutdownFlags = server.shutdown_on_sigterm;
1337
1338        if (prepareForShutdown(shutdownFlags) == C_OK) exit(0);
1339    } else if (isShutdownInitiated()) {
1340        if (server.mstime >= server.shutdown_mstime || isReadyToShutdown()) {
1341            if (finishShutdown() == C_OK) exit(0);
1342            /* Shutdown failed. Continue running. An error has been logged. */
1343        }
1344    }
1345
1346    /* Show some info about non-empty databases */
1347    if (server.verbosity <= LL_VERBOSE) {
1348        run_with_period(5000) {
1349            for (j = 0; j < server.dbnum; j++) {
1350                long long size, used, vkeys;
1351
1352                size = dictSlots(server.db[j].dict);
1353                used = dictSize(server.db[j].dict);
1354                vkeys = dictSize(server.db[j].expires);
1355                if (used || vkeys) {
1356                    serverLog(LL_VERBOSE,"DB %d: %lld keys (%lld volatile) in %lld slots HT.",j,used,vkeys,size);
1357                }
1358            }
1359        }
1360    }
1361
1362    /* Show information about connected clients */
1363    if (!server.sentinel_mode) {
1364        run_with_period(5000) {
1365            serverLog(LL_DEBUG,
1366                "%lu clients connected (%lu replicas), %zu bytes in use",
1367                listLength(server.clients)-listLength(server.slaves),
1368                listLength(server.slaves),
1369                zmalloc_used_memory());
1370        }
1371    }
1372
1373    /* We need to do a few operations on clients asynchronously. */
1374    clientsCron();
1375
1376    /* Handle background operations on Redis databases. */
1377    databasesCron();
1378
1379    /* Start a scheduled AOF rewrite if this was requested by the user while
1380     * a BGSAVE was in progress. */
1381    if (!hasActiveChildProcess() &&
1382        server.aof_rewrite_scheduled &&
1383        !aofRewriteLimited())
1384    {
1385        rewriteAppendOnlyFileBackground();
1386    }
1387
1388    /* Check if a background saving or AOF rewrite in progress terminated. */
1389    if (hasActiveChildProcess() || ldbPendingChildren())
1390    {
1391        run_with_period(1000) receiveChildInfo();
1392        checkChildrenDone();
1393    } else {
1394        /* If there is not a background saving/rewrite in progress check if
1395         * we have to save/rewrite now. */
1396        for (j = 0; j < server.saveparamslen; j++) {
1397            struct saveparam *sp = server.saveparams+j;
1398
1399            /* Save if we reached the given amount of changes,
1400             * the given amount of seconds, and if the latest bgsave was
1401             * successful or if, in case of an error, at least
1402             * CONFIG_BGSAVE_RETRY_DELAY seconds already elapsed. */
1403            if (server.dirty >= sp->changes &&
1404                server.unixtime-server.lastsave > sp->seconds &&
1405                (server.unixtime-server.lastbgsave_try >
1406                 CONFIG_BGSAVE_RETRY_DELAY ||
1407                 server.lastbgsave_status == C_OK))
1408            {
1409                serverLog(LL_NOTICE,"%d changes in %d seconds. Saving...",
1410                    sp->changes, (int)sp->seconds);
1411                rdbSaveInfo rsi, *rsiptr;
1412                rsiptr = rdbPopulateSaveInfo(&rsi);
1413                rdbSaveBackground(SLAVE_REQ_NONE,server.rdb_filename,rsiptr,RDBFLAGS_NONE);
1414                break;
1415            }
1416        }
1417
1418        /* Trigger an AOF rewrite if needed. */
1419        if (server.aof_state == AOF_ON &&
1420            !hasActiveChildProcess() &&
1421            server.aof_rewrite_perc &&
1422            server.aof_current_size > server.aof_rewrite_min_size)
1423        {
1424            long long base = server.aof_rewrite_base_size ?
1425                server.aof_rewrite_base_size : 1;
1426            long long growth = (server.aof_current_size*100/base) - 100;
1427            if (growth >= server.aof_rewrite_perc && !aofRewriteLimited()) {
1428                serverLog(LL_NOTICE,"Starting automatic rewriting of AOF on %lld%% growth",growth);
1429                rewriteAppendOnlyFileBackground();
1430            }
1431        }
1432    }
1433    /* Just for the sake of defensive programming, to avoid forgetting to
1434     * call this function when needed. */
1435    updateDictResizePolicy();
1436
1437
1438    /* AOF postponed flush: Try at every cron cycle if the slow fsync
1439     * completed. */
1440    if ((server.aof_state == AOF_ON || server.aof_state == AOF_WAIT_REWRITE) &&
1441        server.aof_flush_postponed_start)
1442    {
1443        flushAppendOnlyFile(0);
1444    }
1445
1446    /* AOF write errors: in this case we have a buffer to flush as well and
1447     * clear the AOF error in case of success to make the DB writable again,
1448     * however to try every second is enough in case of 'hz' is set to
1449     * a higher frequency. */
1450    run_with_period(1000) {
1451        if ((server.aof_state == AOF_ON || server.aof_state == AOF_WAIT_REWRITE) &&
1452            server.aof_last_write_status == C_ERR) 
1453            {
1454                flushAppendOnlyFile(0);
1455            }
1456    }
1457
1458    /* Clear the paused actions state if needed. */
1459    updatePausedActions();
1460
1461    /* Replication cron function -- used to reconnect to master,
1462     * detect transfer failures, start background RDB transfers and so forth. 
1463     * 
1464     * If Redis is trying to failover then run the replication cron faster so
1465     * progress on the handshake happens more quickly. */
1466    if (server.failover_state != NO_FAILOVER) {
1467        run_with_period(100) replicationCron();
1468    } else {
1469        run_with_period(1000) replicationCron();
1470    }
1471
1472    /* Run the Redis Cluster cron. */
1473    run_with_period(100) {
1474        if (server.cluster_enabled) clusterCron();
1475    }
1476
1477    /* Run the Sentinel timer if we are in sentinel mode. */
1478    if (server.sentinel_mode) sentinelTimer();
1479
1480    /* Cleanup expired MIGRATE cached sockets. */
1481    run_with_period(1000) {
1482        migrateCloseTimedoutSockets();
1483    }
1484
1485    /* Stop the I/O threads if we don't have enough pending work. */
1486    stopThreadedIOIfNeeded();
1487
1488    /* Resize tracking keys table if needed. This is also done at every
1489     * command execution, but we want to be sure that if the last command
1490     * executed changes the value via CONFIG SET, the server will perform
1491     * the operation even if completely idle. */
1492    if (server.tracking_clients) trackingLimitUsedSlots();
1493
1494    /* Start a scheduled BGSAVE if the corresponding flag is set. This is
1495     * useful when we are forced to postpone a BGSAVE because an AOF
1496     * rewrite is in progress.
1497     *
1498     * Note: this code must be after the replicationCron() call above so
1499     * make sure when refactoring this file to keep this order. This is useful
1500     * because we want to give priority to RDB savings for replication. */
1501    if (!hasActiveChildProcess() &&
1502        server.rdb_bgsave_scheduled &&
1503        (server.unixtime-server.lastbgsave_try > CONFIG_BGSAVE_RETRY_DELAY ||
1504         server.lastbgsave_status == C_OK))
1505    {
1506        rdbSaveInfo rsi, *rsiptr;
1507        rsiptr = rdbPopulateSaveInfo(&rsi);
1508        if (rdbSaveBackground(SLAVE_REQ_NONE,server.rdb_filename,rsiptr,RDBFLAGS_NONE) == C_OK)
1509            server.rdb_bgsave_scheduled = 0;
1510    }
1511
1512    run_with_period(100) {
1513        if (moduleCount()) modulesCron();
1514    }
1515
1516    /* Fire the cron loop modules event. */
1517    RedisModuleCronLoopV1 ei = {REDISMODULE_CRON_LOOP_VERSION,server.hz};
1518    moduleFireServerEvent(REDISMODULE_EVENT_CRON_LOOP,
1519                          0,
1520                          &ei);
1521
1522    server.cronloops++;
1523
1524    server.el_cron_duration = getMonotonicUs() - cron_start;
1525
1526    return 1000/server.hz;
1527}
1528
1529
1530void blockingOperationStarts(void) {
1531    if(!server.blocking_op_nesting++){
1532        updateCachedTime(0);
1533        server.blocked_last_cron = server.mstime;
1534    }
1535}
1536
1537void blockingOperationEnds(void) {
1538    if(!(--server.blocking_op_nesting)){
1539        server.blocked_last_cron = 0;
1540    }
1541}
1542
1543/* This function fills in the role of serverCron during RDB or AOF loading, and
1544 * also during blocked scripts.
1545 * It attempts to do its duties at a similar rate as the configured server.hz,
1546 * and updates cronloops variable so that similarly to serverCron, the
1547 * run_with_period can be used. */
1548void whileBlockedCron(void) {
1549    /* Here we may want to perform some cron jobs (normally done server.hz times
1550     * per second). */
1551
1552    /* Since this function depends on a call to blockingOperationStarts, let's
1553     * make sure it was done. */
1554    serverAssert(server.blocked_last_cron);
1555
1556    /* In case we where called too soon, leave right away. This way one time
1557     * jobs after the loop below don't need an if. and we don't bother to start
1558     * latency monitor if this function is called too often. */
1559    if (server.blocked_last_cron >= server.mstime)
1560        return;
1561
1562    mstime_t latency;
1563    latencyStartMonitor(latency);
1564
1565    /* In some cases we may be called with big intervals, so we may need to do
1566     * extra work here. This is because some of the functions in serverCron rely
1567     * on the fact that it is performed every 10 ms or so. For instance, if
1568     * activeDefragCycle needs to utilize 25% cpu, it will utilize 2.5ms, so we
1569     * need to call it multiple times. */
1570    long hz_ms = 1000/server.hz;
1571    while (server.blocked_last_cron < server.mstime) {
1572
1573        /* Defrag keys gradually. */
1574        activeDefragCycle();
1575
1576        server.blocked_last_cron += hz_ms;
1577
1578        /* Increment cronloop so that run_with_period works. */
1579        server.cronloops++;
1580    }
1581
1582    /* Other cron jobs do not need to be done in a loop. No need to check
1583     * server.blocked_last_cron since we have an early exit at the top. */
1584
1585    /* Update memory stats during loading (excluding blocked scripts) */
1586    if (server.loading) cronUpdateMemoryStats();
1587
1588    latencyEndMonitor(latency);
1589    latencyAddSampleIfNeeded("while-blocked-cron",latency);
1590
1591    /* We received a SIGTERM during loading, shutting down here in a safe way,
1592     * as it isn't ok doing so inside the signal handler. */
1593    if (server.shutdown_asap && server.loading) {
1594        if (prepareForShutdown(SHUTDOWN_NOSAVE) == C_OK) exit(0);
1595        serverLog(LL_WARNING,"SIGTERM received but errors trying to shut down the server, check the logs for more information");
1596        server.shutdown_asap = 0;
1597        server.last_sig_received = 0;
1598    }
1599}
1600
1601static void sendGetackToReplicas(void) {
1602    robj *argv[3];
1603    argv[0] = shared.replconf;
1604    argv[1] = shared.getack;
1605    argv[2] = shared.special_asterick; /* Not used argument. */
1606    replicationFeedSlaves(server.slaves, -1, argv, 3);
1607}
1608
1609extern int ProcessingEventsWhileBlocked;
1610
1611/* This function gets called every time Redis is entering the
1612 * main loop of the event driven library, that is, before to sleep
1613 * for ready file descriptors.
1614 *
1615 * Note: This function is (currently) called from two functions:
1616 * 1. aeMain - The main server loop
1617 * 2. processEventsWhileBlocked - Process clients during RDB/AOF load
1618 *
1619 * If it was called from processEventsWhileBlocked we don't want
1620 * to perform all actions (For example, we don't want to expire
1621 * keys), but we do need to perform some actions.
1622 *
1623 * The most important is freeClientsInAsyncFreeQueue but we also
1624 * call some other low-risk functions. */
1625void beforeSleep(struct aeEventLoop *eventLoop) {
1626    UNUSED(eventLoop);
1627
1628    size_t zmalloc_used = zmalloc_used_memory();
1629    if (zmalloc_used > server.stat_peak_memory)
1630        server.stat_peak_memory = zmalloc_used;
1631
1632    /* Just call a subset of vital functions in case we are re-entering
1633     * the event loop from processEventsWhileBlocked(). Note that in this
1634     * case we keep track of the number of events we are processing, since
1635     * processEventsWhileBlocked() wants to stop ASAP if there are no longer
1636     * events to handle. */
1637    if (ProcessingEventsWhileBlocked) {
1638        uint64_t processed = 0;
1639        processed += handleClientsWithPendingReadsUsingThreads();
1640        processed += connTypeProcessPendingData();
1641        if (server.aof_state == AOF_ON || server.aof_state == AOF_WAIT_REWRITE)
1642            flushAppendOnlyFile(0);
1643        processed += handleClientsWithPendingWrites();
1644        processed += freeClientsInAsyncFreeQueue();
1645        server.events_processed_while_blocked += processed;
1646        return;
1647    }
1648
1649    /* We should handle pending reads clients ASAP after event loop. */
1650    handleClientsWithPendingReadsUsingThreads();
1651
1652    /* Handle pending data(typical TLS). (must be done before flushAppendOnlyFile) */
1653    connTypeProcessPendingData();
1654
1655    /* If any connection type(typical TLS) still has pending unread data don't sleep at all. */
1656    aeSetDontWait(server.el, connTypeHasPendingData());
1657
1658    /* Call the Redis Cluster before sleep function. Note that this function
1659     * may change the state of Redis Cluster (from ok to fail or vice versa),
1660     * so it's a good idea to call it before serving the unblocked clients
1661     * later in this function, must be done before blockedBeforeSleep. */
1662    if (server.cluster_enabled) clusterBeforeSleep();
1663
1664    /* Handle blocked clients.
1665     * must be done before flushAppendOnlyFile, in case of appendfsync=always,
1666     * since the unblocked clients may write data. */
1667    blockedBeforeSleep();
1668
1669    /* Record cron time in beforeSleep, which is the sum of active-expire, active-defrag and all other
1670     * tasks done by cron and beforeSleep, but excluding read, write and AOF, that are counted by other
1671     * sets of metrics. */
1672    monotime cron_start_time_before_aof = getMonotonicUs();
1673
1674    /* Run a fast expire cycle (the called function will return
1675     * ASAP if a fast cycle is not needed). */
1676    if (server.active_expire_enabled && iAmMaster())
1677        activeExpireCycle(ACTIVE_EXPIRE_CYCLE_FAST);
1678
1679    if (moduleCount()) {
1680        moduleFireServerEvent(REDISMODULE_EVENT_EVENTLOOP,
1681                              REDISMODULE_SUBEVENT_EVENTLOOP_BEFORE_SLEEP,
1682                              NULL);
1683    }
1684
1685    /* Send all the slaves an ACK request if at least one client blocked
1686     * during the previous event loop iteration. Note that we do this after
1687     * processUnblockedClients(), so if there are multiple pipelined WAITs
1688     * and the just unblocked WAIT gets blocked again, we don't have to wait
1689     * a server cron cycle in absence of other event loop events. See #6623.
1690     * 
1691     * We also don't send the ACKs while clients are paused, since it can
1692     * increment the replication backlog, they'll be sent after the pause
1693     * if we are still the master. */
1694    if (server.get_ack_from_slaves && !isPausedActionsWithUpdate(PAUSE_ACTION_REPLICA)) {
1695        sendGetackToReplicas();
1696        server.get_ack_from_slaves = 0;
1697    }
1698
1699    /* We may have received updates from clients about their current offset. NOTE:
1700     * this can't be done where the ACK is received since failover will disconnect 
1701     * our clients. */
1702    updateFailoverStatus();
1703
1704    /* Since we rely on current_client to send scheduled invalidation messages
1705     * we have to flush them after each command, so when we get here, the list
1706     * must be empty. */
1707    serverAssert(listLength(server.tracking_pending_keys) == 0);
1708    serverAssert(listLength(server.pending_push_messages) == 0);
1709
1710    /* Send the invalidation messages to clients participating to the
1711     * client side caching protocol in broadcasting (BCAST) mode. */
1712    trackingBroadcastInvalidationMessages();
1713
1714    /* Record time consumption of AOF writing. */
1715    monotime aof_start_time = getMonotonicUs();
1716    /* Record cron time in beforeSleep. This does not include the time consumed by AOF writing and IO writing below. */
1717    monotime duration_before_aof = aof_start_time - cron_start_time_before_aof;
1718
1719    /* Write the AOF buffer on disk,
1720     * must be done before handleClientsWithPendingWritesUsingThreads,
1721     * in case of appendfsync=always. */
1722    if (server.aof_state == AOF_ON || server.aof_state == AOF_WAIT_REWRITE)
1723        flushAppendOnlyFile(0);
1724
1725    /* Record time consumption of AOF writing. */
1726    durationAddSample(EL_DURATION_TYPE_AOF, getMonotonicUs() - aof_start_time);
1727
1728    /* Update the fsynced replica offset.
1729     * If an initial rewrite is in progress then not all data is guaranteed to have actually been
1730     * persisted to disk yet, so we cannot update the field. We will wait for the rewrite to complete. */
1731    if (server.aof_state == AOF_ON && server.fsynced_reploff != -1) {
1732        long long fsynced_reploff_pending;
1733        atomicGet(server.fsynced_reploff_pending, fsynced_reploff_pending);
1734        server.fsynced_reploff = fsynced_reploff_pending;
1735    }
1736
1737    /* Handle writes with pending output buffers. */
1738    handleClientsWithPendingWritesUsingThreads();
1739
1740    /* Record cron time in beforeSleep. This does not include the time consumed by AOF writing and IO writing above. */
1741    monotime cron_start_time_after_write = getMonotonicUs();
1742
1743    /* Close clients that need to be closed asynchronous */
1744    freeClientsInAsyncFreeQueue();
1745
1746    /* Incrementally trim replication backlog, 10 times the normal speed is
1747     * to free replication backlog as much as possible. */
1748    if (server.repl_backlog)
1749        incrementalTrimReplicationBacklog(10*REPL_BACKLOG_TRIM_BLOCKS_PER_CALL);
1750
1751    /* Disconnect some clients if they are consuming too much memory. */
1752    evictClients();
1753
1754    /* Record cron time in beforeSleep. */
1755    monotime duration_after_write = getMonotonicUs() - cron_start_time_after_write;
1756
1757    /* Record eventloop latency. */
1758    if (server.el_start > 0) {
1759        monotime el_duration = getMonotonicUs() - server.el_start;
1760        durationAddSample(EL_DURATION_TYPE_EL, el_duration);
1761    }
1762    server.el_cron_duration += duration_before_aof + duration_after_write;
1763    durationAddSample(EL_DURATION_TYPE_CRON, server.el_cron_duration);
1764    server.el_cron_duration = 0;
1765    /* Record max command count per cycle. */
1766    if (server.stat_numcommands > server.el_cmd_cnt_start) {
1767        long long el_command_cnt = server.stat_numcommands - server.el_cmd_cnt_start;
1768        if (el_command_cnt > server.el_cmd_cnt_max) {
1769            server.el_cmd_cnt_max = el_command_cnt;
1770        }
1771    }
1772
1773    /* Before we are going to sleep, let the threads access the dataset by
1774     * releasing the GIL. Redis main thread will not touch anything at this
1775     * time. */
1776    if (moduleCount()) moduleReleaseGIL();
1777    /********************* WARNING ********************
1778     * Do NOT add anything below moduleReleaseGIL !!! *
1779     ***************************** ********************/
1780}
1781
1782/* This function is called immediately after the event loop multiplexing
1783 * API returned, and the control is going to soon return to Redis by invoking
1784 * the different events callbacks. */
1785void afterSleep(struct aeEventLoop *eventLoop) {
1786    UNUSED(eventLoop);
1787    /********************* WARNING ********************
1788     * Do NOT add anything above moduleAcquireGIL !!! *
1789     ***************************** ********************/
1790    if (!ProcessingEventsWhileBlocked) {
1791        /* Acquire the modules GIL so that their threads won't touch anything. */
1792        if (moduleCount()) {
1793            mstime_t latency;
1794            latencyStartMonitor(latency);
1795
1796            moduleAcquireGIL();
1797            moduleFireServerEvent(REDISMODULE_EVENT_EVENTLOOP,
1798                                  REDISMODULE_SUBEVENT_EVENTLOOP_AFTER_SLEEP,
1799                                  NULL);
1800            latencyEndMonitor(latency);
1801            latencyAddSampleIfNeeded("module-acquire-GIL",latency);
1802        }
1803        /* Set the eventloop start time. */
1804        server.el_start = getMonotonicUs();
1805        /* Set the eventloop command count at start. */
1806        server.el_cmd_cnt_start = server.stat_numcommands;
1807    }
1808
1809    /* Update the time cache. */
1810    updateCachedTime(1);
1811
1812    /* Update command time snapshot in case it'll be required without a command
1813     * e.g. somehow used by module timers. Don't update it while yielding to a
1814     * blocked command, call() will handle that and restore the original time. */
1815    if (!ProcessingEventsWhileBlocked) {
1816        server.cmd_time_snapshot = server.mstime;
1817    }
1818}

1820/* =========================== Server initialization ======================== */
