3113/* ====================== Commands lookup and execution ===================== */
3114
3115int isContainerCommandBySds(sds s) {
3116    struct redisCommand *base_cmd = dictFetchValue(server.commands, s);
3117    int has_subcommands = base_cmd && base_cmd->subcommands_dict;
3118    return has_subcommands;
3119}
3120
3121struct redisCommand *lookupSubcommand(struct redisCommand *container, sds sub_name) {
3122    return dictFetchValue(container->subcommands_dict, sub_name);
3123}
3124
3125/* Look up a command by argv and argc
3126 *
3127 * If `strict` is not 0 we expect argc to be exact (i.e. argc==2
3128 * for a subcommand and argc==1 for a top-level command)
3129 * `strict` should be used every time we want to look up a command
3130 * name (e.g. in COMMAND INFO) rather than to find the command
3131 * a user requested to execute (in processCommand).
3132 */
3133struct redisCommand *lookupCommandLogic(dict *commands, robj **argv, int argc, int strict) {
3134    struct redisCommand *base_cmd = dictFetchValue(commands, argv[0]->ptr);
3135    int has_subcommands = base_cmd && base_cmd->subcommands_dict;
3136    if (argc == 1 || !has_subcommands) {
3137        if (strict && argc != 1)
3138            return NULL;
3139        /* Note: It is possible that base_cmd->proc==NULL (e.g. CONFIG) */
3140        return base_cmd;
3141    } else { /* argc > 1 && has_subcommands */
3142        if (strict && argc != 2)
3143            return NULL;
3144        /* Note: Currently we support just one level of subcommands */
3145        return lookupSubcommand(base_cmd, argv[1]->ptr);
3146    }
3147}
3148
3149struct redisCommand *lookupCommand(robj **argv, int argc) {
3150    return lookupCommandLogic(server.commands,argv,argc,0);
3151}
3152
3153struct redisCommand *lookupCommandBySdsLogic(dict *commands, sds s) {
3154    int argc, j;
3155    sds *strings = sdssplitlen(s,sdslen(s),"|",1,&argc);
3156    if (strings == NULL)
3157        return NULL;
3158    if (argc < 1 || argc > 2) {
3159        /* Currently we support just one level of subcommands */
3160        sdsfreesplitres(strings,argc);
3161        return NULL;
3162    }
3163
3164    serverAssert(argc > 0); /* Avoid warning `-Wmaybe-uninitialized` in lookupCommandLogic() */
3165    robj objects[argc];
3166    robj *argv[argc];
3167    for (j = 0; j < argc; j++) {
3168        initStaticStringObject(objects[j],strings[j]);
3169        argv[j] = &objects[j];
3170    }
3171
3172    struct redisCommand *cmd = lookupCommandLogic(commands,argv,argc,1);
3173    sdsfreesplitres(strings,argc);
3174    return cmd;
3175}
3176
3177struct redisCommand *lookupCommandBySds(sds s) {
3178    return lookupCommandBySdsLogic(server.commands,s);
3179}
3180
3181struct redisCommand *lookupCommandByCStringLogic(dict *commands, const char *s) {
3182    struct redisCommand *cmd;
3183    sds name = sdsnew(s);
3184
3185    cmd = lookupCommandBySdsLogic(commands,name);
3186    sdsfree(name);
3187    return cmd;
3188}
3189
3190struct redisCommand *lookupCommandByCString(const char *s) {
3191    return lookupCommandByCStringLogic(server.commands,s);
3192}
3193
3194/* Lookup the command in the current table, if not found also check in
3195 * the original table containing the original command names unaffected by
3196 * redis.conf rename-command statement.
3197 *
3198 * This is used by functions rewriting the argument vector such as
3199 * rewriteClientCommandVector() in order to set client->cmd pointer
3200 * correctly even if the command was renamed. */
3201struct redisCommand *lookupCommandOrOriginal(robj **argv ,int argc) {
3202    struct redisCommand *cmd = lookupCommandLogic(server.commands, argv, argc, 0);
3203
3204    if (!cmd) cmd = lookupCommandLogic(server.orig_commands, argv, argc, 0);
3205    return cmd;
3206}
3207
3208/* Commands arriving from the master client or AOF client, should never be rejected. */
3209int mustObeyClient(client *c) {
3210    return c->id == CLIENT_ID_AOF || c->flags & CLIENT_MASTER;
3211}
3212
3213static int shouldPropagate(int target) {
3214    if (!server.replication_allowed || target == PROPAGATE_NONE || server.loading)
3215        return 0;
3216
3217    if (target & PROPAGATE_AOF) {
3218        if (server.aof_state != AOF_OFF)
3219            return 1;
3220    }
3221    if (target & PROPAGATE_REPL) {
3222        if (server.masterhost == NULL && (server.repl_backlog || listLength(server.slaves) != 0))
3223            return 1;
3224    }
3225
3226    return 0;
3227}
3228
3229/* Propagate the specified command (in the context of the specified database id)
3230 * to AOF and Slaves.
3231 *
3232 * flags are an xor between:
3233 * + PROPAGATE_NONE (no propagation of command at all)
3234 * + PROPAGATE_AOF (propagate into the AOF file if is enabled)
3235 * + PROPAGATE_REPL (propagate into the replication link)
3236 *
3237 * This is an internal low-level function and should not be called!
3238 *
3239 * The API for propagating commands is alsoPropagate().
3240 *
3241 * dbid value of -1 is saved to indicate that the called do not want
3242 * to replicate SELECT for this command (used for database neutral commands).
3243 */
3244static void propagateNow(int dbid, robj **argv, int argc, int target) {
3245    if (!shouldPropagate(target))
3246        return;
3247
3248    /* This needs to be unreachable since the dataset should be fixed during
3249     * replica pause (otherwise data may be lost during a failover) */
3250    serverAssert(!(isPausedActions(PAUSE_ACTION_REPLICA) &&
3251                   (!server.client_pause_in_transaction)));
3252
3253    if (server.aof_state != AOF_OFF && target & PROPAGATE_AOF)
3254        feedAppendOnlyFile(dbid,argv,argc);
3255    if (target & PROPAGATE_REPL)
3256        replicationFeedSlaves(server.slaves,dbid,argv,argc);
3257}
3258
3259/* Used inside commands to schedule the propagation of additional commands
3260 * after the current command is propagated to AOF / Replication.
3261 *
3262 * dbid is the database ID the command should be propagated into.
3263 * Arguments of the command to propagate are passed as an array of redis
3264 * objects pointers of len 'argc', using the 'argv' vector.
3265 *
3266 * The function does not take a reference to the passed 'argv' vector,
3267 * so it is up to the caller to release the passed argv (but it is usually
3268 * stack allocated).  The function automatically increments ref count of
3269 * passed objects, so the caller does not need to. */
3270void alsoPropagate(int dbid, robj **argv, int argc, int target) {
3271    robj **argvcopy;
3272    int j;
3273
3274    if (!shouldPropagate(target))
3275        return;
3276
3277    argvcopy = zmalloc(sizeof(robj*)*argc);
3278    for (j = 0; j < argc; j++) {
3279        argvcopy[j] = argv[j];
3280        incrRefCount(argv[j]);
3281    }
3282    redisOpArrayAppend(&server.also_propagate,dbid,argvcopy,argc,target);
3283}
3284
3285/* It is possible to call the function forceCommandPropagation() inside a
3286 * Redis command implementation in order to to force the propagation of a
3287 * specific command execution into AOF / Replication. */
3288void forceCommandPropagation(client *c, int flags) {
3289    serverAssert(c->cmd->flags & (CMD_WRITE | CMD_MAY_REPLICATE));
3290    if (flags & PROPAGATE_REPL) c->flags |= CLIENT_FORCE_REPL;
3291    if (flags & PROPAGATE_AOF) c->flags |= CLIENT_FORCE_AOF;
3292}
3293
3294/* Avoid that the executed command is propagated at all. This way we
3295 * are free to just propagate what we want using the alsoPropagate()
3296 * API. */
3297void preventCommandPropagation(client *c) {
3298    c->flags |= CLIENT_PREVENT_PROP;
3299}
3300
3301/* AOF specific version of preventCommandPropagation(). */
3302void preventCommandAOF(client *c) {
3303    c->flags |= CLIENT_PREVENT_AOF_PROP;
3304}
3305
3306/* Replication specific version of preventCommandPropagation(). */
3307void preventCommandReplication(client *c) {
3308    c->flags |= CLIENT_PREVENT_REPL_PROP;
3309}
3310
3311/* Log the last command a client executed into the slowlog. */
3312void slowlogPushCurrentCommand(client *c, struct redisCommand *cmd, ustime_t duration) {
3313    /* Some commands may contain sensitive data that should not be available in the slowlog. */
3314    if (cmd->flags & CMD_SKIP_SLOWLOG)
3315        return;
3316
3317    /* If command argument vector was rewritten, use the original
3318     * arguments. */
3319    robj **argv = c->original_argv ? c->original_argv : c->argv;
3320    int argc = c->original_argv ? c->original_argc : c->argc;
3321    slowlogPushEntryIfNeeded(c,argv,argc,duration);
3322}
3323
3324/* This function is called in order to update the total command histogram duration.
3325 * The latency unit is nano-seconds.
3326 * If needed it will allocate the histogram memory and trim the duration to the upper/lower tracking limits*/
3327void updateCommandLatencyHistogram(struct hdr_histogram **latency_histogram, int64_t duration_hist){
3328    if (duration_hist < LATENCY_HISTOGRAM_MIN_VALUE)
3329        duration_hist=LATENCY_HISTOGRAM_MIN_VALUE;
3330    if (duration_hist>LATENCY_HISTOGRAM_MAX_VALUE)
3331        duration_hist=LATENCY_HISTOGRAM_MAX_VALUE;
3332    if (*latency_histogram==NULL)
3333        hdr_init(LATENCY_HISTOGRAM_MIN_VALUE,LATENCY_HISTOGRAM_MAX_VALUE,LATENCY_HISTOGRAM_PRECISION,latency_histogram);
3334    hdr_record_value(*latency_histogram,duration_hist);
3335}
3336
3337/* Handle the alsoPropagate() API to handle commands that want to propagate
3338 * multiple separated commands. Note that alsoPropagate() is not affected
3339 * by CLIENT_PREVENT_PROP flag. */
3340static void propagatePendingCommands(void) {
3341    if (server.also_propagate.numops == 0)
3342        return;
3343
3344    int j;
3345    redisOp *rop;
3346
3347    /* If we got here it means we have finished an execution-unit.
3348     * If that unit has caused propagation of multiple commands, they
3349     * should be propagated as a transaction */
3350    int transaction = server.also_propagate.numops > 1;
3351
3352    /* In case a command that may modify random keys was run *directly*
3353     * (i.e. not from within a script, MULTI/EXEC, RM_Call, etc.) we want
3354     * to avoid using a transaction (much like active-expire) */
3355    if (server.current_client &&
3356        server.current_client->cmd &&
3357        server.current_client->cmd->flags & CMD_TOUCHES_ARBITRARY_KEYS)
3358    {
3359        transaction = 0;
3360    }
3361
3362    if (transaction) {
3363        /* We use dbid=-1 to indicate we do not want to replicate SELECT.
3364         * It'll be inserted together with the next command (inside the MULTI) */
3365        propagateNow(-1,&shared.multi,1,PROPAGATE_AOF|PROPAGATE_REPL);
3366    }
3367
3368    for (j = 0; j < server.also_propagate.numops; j++) {
3369        rop = &server.also_propagate.ops[j];
3370        serverAssert(rop->target);
3371        propagateNow(rop->dbid,rop->argv,rop->argc,rop->target);
3372    }
3373
3374    if (transaction) {
3375        /* We use dbid=-1 to indicate we do not want to replicate select */
3376        propagateNow(-1,&shared.exec,1,PROPAGATE_AOF|PROPAGATE_REPL);
3377    }
3378
3379    redisOpArrayFree(&server.also_propagate);
3380}
3381
3382/* Performs operations that should be performed after an execution unit ends.
3383 * Execution unit is a code that should be done atomically.
3384 * Execution units can be nested and are not necessarily starts with Redis command.
3385 *
3386 * For example the following is a logical unit:
3387 *   active expire ->
3388 *      trigger del notification of some module ->
3389 *          accessing a key ->
3390 *              trigger key miss notification of some other module
3391 *
3392 * What we want to achieve is that the entire execution unit will be done atomically,
3393 * currently with respect to replication and post jobs, but in the future there might
3394 * be other considerations. So we basically want the `postUnitOperations` to trigger
3395 * after the entire chain finished. */
3396void postExecutionUnitOperations(void) {
3397    if (server.execution_nesting)
3398        return;
3399
3400    firePostExecutionUnitJobs();
3401
3402    /* If we are at the top-most call() and not inside a an active module
3403     * context (e.g. within a module timer) we can propagate what we accumulated. */
3404    propagatePendingCommands();
3405
3406    /* Module subsystem post-execution-unit logic */
3407    modulePostExecutionUnitOperations();
3408}
3409
3410/* Increment the command failure counters (either rejected_calls or failed_calls).
3411 * The decision which counter to increment is done using the flags argument, options are:
3412 * * ERROR_COMMAND_REJECTED - update rejected_calls
3413 * * ERROR_COMMAND_FAILED - update failed_calls
3414 *
3415 * The function also reset the prev_err_count to make sure we will not count the same error
3416 * twice, its possible to pass a NULL cmd value to indicate that the error was counted elsewhere.
3417 *
3418 * The function returns true if stats was updated and false if not. */
3419int incrCommandStatsOnError(struct redisCommand *cmd, int flags) {
3420    /* hold the prev error count captured on the last command execution */
3421    static long long prev_err_count = 0;
3422    int res = 0;
3423    if (cmd) {
3424        if ((server.stat_total_error_replies - prev_err_count) > 0) {
3425            if (flags & ERROR_COMMAND_REJECTED) {
3426                cmd->rejected_calls++;
3427                res = 1;
3428            } else if (flags & ERROR_COMMAND_FAILED) {
3429                cmd->failed_calls++;
3430                res = 1;
3431            }
3432        }
3433    }
3434    prev_err_count = server.stat_total_error_replies;
3435    return res;
3436}
3437
3438/* Call() is the core of Redis execution of a command.
3439 *
3440 * The following flags can be passed:
3441 * CMD_CALL_NONE        No flags.
3442 * CMD_CALL_PROPAGATE_AOF   Append command to AOF if it modified the dataset
3443 *                          or if the client flags are forcing propagation.
3444 * CMD_CALL_PROPAGATE_REPL  Send command to slaves if it modified the dataset
3445 *                          or if the client flags are forcing propagation.
3446 * CMD_CALL_PROPAGATE   Alias for PROPAGATE_AOF|PROPAGATE_REPL.
3447 * CMD_CALL_FULL        Alias for SLOWLOG|STATS|PROPAGATE.
3448 *
3449 * The exact propagation behavior depends on the client flags.
3450 * Specifically:
3451 *
3452 * 1. If the client flags CLIENT_FORCE_AOF or CLIENT_FORCE_REPL are set
3453 *    and assuming the corresponding CMD_CALL_PROPAGATE_AOF/REPL is set
3454 *    in the call flags, then the command is propagated even if the
3455 *    dataset was not affected by the command.
3456 * 2. If the client flags CLIENT_PREVENT_REPL_PROP or CLIENT_PREVENT_AOF_PROP
3457 *    are set, the propagation into AOF or to slaves is not performed even
3458 *    if the command modified the dataset.
3459 *
3460 * Note that regardless of the client flags, if CMD_CALL_PROPAGATE_AOF
3461 * or CMD_CALL_PROPAGATE_REPL are not set, then respectively AOF or
3462 * slaves propagation will never occur.
3463 *
3464 * Client flags are modified by the implementation of a given command
3465 * using the following API:
3466 *
3467 * forceCommandPropagation(client *c, int flags);
3468 * preventCommandPropagation(client *c);
3469 * preventCommandAOF(client *c);
3470 * preventCommandReplication(client *c);
3471 *
3472 */
3473void call(client *c, int flags) {
3474    long long dirty;
3475    uint64_t client_old_flags = c->flags;
3476    struct redisCommand *real_cmd = c->realcmd;
3477    client *prev_client = server.executing_client;
3478    server.executing_client = c;
3479
3480    /* When call() is issued during loading the AOF we don't want commands called
3481     * from module, exec or LUA to go into the slowlog or to populate statistics. */
3482    int update_command_stats = !isAOFLoadingContext();
3483
3484    /* We want to be aware of a client which is making a first time attempt to execute this command
3485     * and a client which is reprocessing command again (after being unblocked).
3486     * Blocked clients can be blocked in different places and not always it means the call() function has been
3487     * called. For example this is required for avoiding double logging to monitors.*/
3488    int reprocessing_command = flags & CMD_CALL_REPROCESSING;
3489
3490    /* Initialization: clear the flags that must be set by the command on
3491     * demand, and initialize the array for additional commands propagation. */
3492    c->flags &= ~(CLIENT_FORCE_AOF|CLIENT_FORCE_REPL|CLIENT_PREVENT_PROP);
3493
3494    /* Redis core is in charge of propagation when the first entry point
3495     * of call() is processCommand().
3496     * The only other option to get to call() without having processCommand
3497     * as an entry point is if a module triggers RM_Call outside of call()
3498     * context (for example, in a timer).
3499     * In that case, the module is in charge of propagation. */
3500
3501    /* Call the command. */
3502    dirty = server.dirty;
3503    long long old_master_repl_offset = server.master_repl_offset;
3504    incrCommandStatsOnError(NULL, 0);
3505
3506    const long long call_timer = ustime();
3507    enterExecutionUnit(1, call_timer);
3508
3509    /* setting the CLIENT_EXECUTING_COMMAND flag so we will avoid
3510     * sending client side caching message in the middle of a command reply.
3511     * In case of blocking commands, the flag will be un-set only after successfully
3512     * re-processing and unblock the client.*/
3513    c->flags |= CLIENT_EXECUTING_COMMAND;
3514
3515    /* Setting the CLIENT_REPROCESSING_COMMAND flag so that during the actual
3516     * processing of the command proc, the client is aware that it is being
3517     * re-processed. */
3518    if (reprocessing_command) c->flags |= CLIENT_REPROCESSING_COMMAND;
3519
3520    monotime monotonic_start = 0;
3521    if (monotonicGetType() == MONOTONIC_CLOCK_HW)
3522        monotonic_start = getMonotonicUs();
3523
3524    c->cmd->proc(c);
3525
3526    /* Clear the CLIENT_REPROCESSING_COMMAND flag after the proc is executed. */
3527    if (reprocessing_command) c->flags &= ~CLIENT_REPROCESSING_COMMAND;
3528
3529    exitExecutionUnit();
3530
3531    /* In case client is blocked after trying to execute the command,
3532     * it means the execution is not yet completed and we MIGHT reprocess the command in the future. */
3533    if (!(c->flags & CLIENT_BLOCKED)) c->flags &= ~(CLIENT_EXECUTING_COMMAND);
3534
3535    /* In order to avoid performance implication due to querying the clock using a system call 3 times,
3536     * we use a monotonic clock, when we are sure its cost is very low, and fall back to non-monotonic call otherwise. */
3537    ustime_t duration;
3538    if (monotonicGetType() == MONOTONIC_CLOCK_HW)
3539        duration = getMonotonicUs() - monotonic_start;
3540    else
3541        duration = ustime() - call_timer;
3542
3543    c->duration += duration;
3544    dirty = server.dirty-dirty;
3545    if (dirty < 0) dirty = 0;
3546
3547    /* Update failed command calls if required. */
3548
3549    if (!incrCommandStatsOnError(real_cmd, ERROR_COMMAND_FAILED) && c->deferred_reply_errors) {
3550        /* When call is used from a module client, error stats, and total_error_replies
3551         * isn't updated since these errors, if handled by the module, are internal,
3552         * and not reflected to users. however, the commandstats does show these calls
3553         * (made by RM_Call), so it should log if they failed or succeeded. */
3554        real_cmd->failed_calls++;
3555    }
3556
3557    /* After executing command, we will close the client after writing entire
3558     * reply if it is set 'CLIENT_CLOSE_AFTER_COMMAND' flag. */
3559    if (c->flags & CLIENT_CLOSE_AFTER_COMMAND) {
3560        c->flags &= ~CLIENT_CLOSE_AFTER_COMMAND;
3561        c->flags |= CLIENT_CLOSE_AFTER_REPLY;
3562    }
3563
3564    /* Note: the code below uses the real command that was executed
3565     * c->cmd and c->lastcmd may be different, in case of MULTI-EXEC or
3566     * re-written commands such as EXPIRE, GEOADD, etc. */
3567
3568    /* Record the latency this command induced on the main thread.
3569     * unless instructed by the caller not to log. (happens when processing
3570     * a MULTI-EXEC from inside an AOF). */
3571    if (update_command_stats) {
3572        char *latency_event = (real_cmd->flags & CMD_FAST) ?
3573                               "fast-command" : "command";
3574        latencyAddSampleIfNeeded(latency_event,duration/1000);
3575        if (server.execution_nesting == 0)
3576            durationAddSample(EL_DURATION_TYPE_CMD, duration);
3577    }
3578
3579    /* Log the command into the Slow log if needed.
3580     * If the client is blocked we will handle slowlog when it is unblocked. */
3581    if (update_command_stats && !(c->flags & CLIENT_BLOCKED))
3582        slowlogPushCurrentCommand(c, real_cmd, c->duration);
3583
3584    /* Send the command to clients in MONITOR mode if applicable,
3585     * since some administrative commands are considered too dangerous to be shown.
3586     * Other exceptions is a client which is unblocked and retrying to process the command
3587     * or we are currently in the process of loading AOF. */
3588    if (update_command_stats && !reprocessing_command &&
3589        !(c->cmd->flags & (CMD_SKIP_MONITOR|CMD_ADMIN))) {
3590        robj **argv = c->original_argv ? c->original_argv : c->argv;
3591        int argc = c->original_argv ? c->original_argc : c->argc;
3592        replicationFeedMonitors(c,server.monitors,c->db->id,argv,argc);
3593    }
3594
3595    /* Clear the original argv.
3596     * If the client is blocked we will handle slowlog when it is unblocked. */
3597    if (!(c->flags & CLIENT_BLOCKED))
3598        freeClientOriginalArgv(c);
3599
3600    /* populate the per-command statistics that we show in INFO commandstats.
3601     * If the client is blocked we will handle latency stats and duration when it is unblocked. */
3602    if (update_command_stats && !(c->flags & CLIENT_BLOCKED)) {
3603        real_cmd->calls++;
3604        real_cmd->microseconds += c->duration;
3605        if (server.latency_tracking_enabled && !(c->flags & CLIENT_BLOCKED))
3606            updateCommandLatencyHistogram(&(real_cmd->latency_histogram), c->duration*1000);
3607    }
3608
3609    /* The duration needs to be reset after each call except for a blocked command,
3610     * which is expected to record and reset the duration after unblocking. */
3611    if (!(c->flags & CLIENT_BLOCKED)) {
3612        c->duration = 0;
3613    }
3614
3615    /* Propagate the command into the AOF and replication link.
3616     * We never propagate EXEC explicitly, it will be implicitly
3617     * propagated if needed (see propagatePendingCommands).
3618     * Also, module commands take care of themselves */
3619    if (flags & CMD_CALL_PROPAGATE &&
3620        (c->flags & CLIENT_PREVENT_PROP) != CLIENT_PREVENT_PROP &&
3621        c->cmd->proc != execCommand &&
3622        !(c->cmd->flags & CMD_MODULE))
3623    {
3624        int propagate_flags = PROPAGATE_NONE;
3625
3626        /* Check if the command operated changes in the data set. If so
3627         * set for replication / AOF propagation. */
3628        if (dirty) propagate_flags |= (PROPAGATE_AOF|PROPAGATE_REPL);
3629
3630        /* If the client forced AOF / replication of the command, set
3631         * the flags regardless of the command effects on the data set. */
3632        if (c->flags & CLIENT_FORCE_REPL) propagate_flags |= PROPAGATE_REPL;
3633        if (c->flags & CLIENT_FORCE_AOF) propagate_flags |= PROPAGATE_AOF;
3634
3635        /* However prevent AOF / replication propagation if the command
3636         * implementation called preventCommandPropagation() or similar,
3637         * or if we don't have the call() flags to do so. */
3638        if (c->flags & CLIENT_PREVENT_REPL_PROP        ||
3639            c->flags & CLIENT_MODULE_PREVENT_REPL_PROP ||
3640            !(flags & CMD_CALL_PROPAGATE_REPL))
3641                propagate_flags &= ~PROPAGATE_REPL;
3642        if (c->flags & CLIENT_PREVENT_AOF_PROP        ||
3643            c->flags & CLIENT_MODULE_PREVENT_AOF_PROP ||
3644            !(flags & CMD_CALL_PROPAGATE_AOF))
3645                propagate_flags &= ~PROPAGATE_AOF;
3646
3647        /* Call alsoPropagate() only if at least one of AOF / replication
3648         * propagation is needed. */
3649        if (propagate_flags != PROPAGATE_NONE)
3650            alsoPropagate(c->db->id,c->argv,c->argc,propagate_flags);
3651    }
3652
3653    /* Restore the old replication flags, since call() can be executed
3654     * recursively. */
3655    c->flags &= ~(CLIENT_FORCE_AOF|CLIENT_FORCE_REPL|CLIENT_PREVENT_PROP);
3656    c->flags |= client_old_flags &
3657        (CLIENT_FORCE_AOF|CLIENT_FORCE_REPL|CLIENT_PREVENT_PROP);
3658
3659    /* If the client has keys tracking enabled for client side caching,
3660     * make sure to remember the keys it fetched via this command. For read-only
3661     * scripts, don't process the script, only the commands it executes. */
3662    if ((c->cmd->flags & CMD_READONLY) && (c->cmd->proc != evalRoCommand)
3663        && (c->cmd->proc != evalShaRoCommand) && (c->cmd->proc != fcallroCommand))
3664    {
3665        /* We use the tracking flag of the original external client that
3666         * triggered the command, but we take the keys from the actual command
3667         * being executed. */
3668        if (server.current_client &&
3669            (server.current_client->flags & CLIENT_TRACKING) &&
3670            !(server.current_client->flags & CLIENT_TRACKING_BCAST))
3671        {
3672            trackingRememberKeys(server.current_client, c);
3673        }
3674    }
3675
3676    if (!(c->flags & CLIENT_BLOCKED))
3677        server.stat_numcommands++;
3678
3679    /* Record peak memory after each command and before the eviction that runs
3680     * before the next command. */
3681    size_t zmalloc_used = zmalloc_used_memory();
3682    if (zmalloc_used > server.stat_peak_memory)
3683        server.stat_peak_memory = zmalloc_used;
3684
3685    /* Do some maintenance job and cleanup */
3686    afterCommand(c);
3687
3688    /* Remember the replication offset of the client, right after its last
3689     * command that resulted in propagation. */
3690    if (old_master_repl_offset != server.master_repl_offset)
3691        c->woff = server.master_repl_offset;
3692
3693    /* Client pause takes effect after a transaction has finished. This needs
3694     * to be located after everything is propagated. */
3695    if (!server.in_exec && server.client_pause_in_transaction) {
3696        server.client_pause_in_transaction = 0;
3697    }
3698
3699    server.executing_client = prev_client;
3700}
3701
3702/* Used when a command that is ready for execution needs to be rejected, due to
3703 * various pre-execution checks. it returns the appropriate error to the client.
3704 * If there's a transaction is flags it as dirty, and if the command is EXEC,
3705 * it aborts the transaction.
3706 * The duration is reset, since we reject the command, and it did not record.
3707 * Note: 'reply' is expected to end with \r\n */
3708void rejectCommand(client *c, robj *reply) {
3709    flagTransaction(c);
3710    c->duration = 0;
3711    if (c->cmd) c->cmd->rejected_calls++;
3712    if (c->cmd && c->cmd->proc == execCommand) {
3713        execCommandAbort(c, reply->ptr);
3714    } else {
3715        /* using addReplyError* rather than addReply so that the error can be logged. */
3716        addReplyErrorObject(c, reply);
3717    }
3718}
3719
3720void rejectCommandSds(client *c, sds s) {
3721    flagTransaction(c);
3722    c->duration = 0;
3723    if (c->cmd) c->cmd->rejected_calls++;
3724    if (c->cmd && c->cmd->proc == execCommand) {
3725        execCommandAbort(c, s);
3726        sdsfree(s);
3727    } else {
3728        /* The following frees 's'. */
3729        addReplyErrorSds(c, s);
3730    }
3731}
3732
3733void rejectCommandFormat(client *c, const char *fmt, ...) {
3734    va_list ap;
3735    va_start(ap,fmt);
3736    sds s = sdscatvprintf(sdsempty(),fmt,ap);
3737    va_end(ap);
3738    /* Make sure there are no newlines in the string, otherwise invalid protocol
3739     * is emitted (The args come from the user, they may contain any character). */
3740    sdsmapchars(s, "\r\n", "  ",  2);
3741    rejectCommandSds(c, s);
3742}
3743
3744/* This is called after a command in call, we can do some maintenance job in it. */
3745void afterCommand(client *c) {
3746    UNUSED(c);
3747    /* Should be done before trackingHandlePendingKeyInvalidations so that we
3748     * reply to client before invalidating cache (makes more sense) */
3749    postExecutionUnitOperations();
3750
3751    /* Flush pending tracking invalidations. */
3752    trackingHandlePendingKeyInvalidations();
3753
3754    /* Flush other pending push messages. only when we are not in nested call.
3755     * So the messages are not interleaved with transaction response. */
3756    if (!server.execution_nesting)
3757        listJoin(c->reply, server.pending_push_messages);
3758}
3759
3760/* Check if c->cmd exists, fills `err` with details in case it doesn't.
3761 * Return 1 if exists. */
3762int commandCheckExistence(client *c, sds *err) {
3763    if (c->cmd)
3764        return 1;
3765    if (!err)
3766        return 0;
3767    if (isContainerCommandBySds(c->argv[0]->ptr)) {
3768        /* If we can't find the command but argv[0] by itself is a command
3769         * it means we're dealing with an invalid subcommand. Print Help. */
3770        sds cmd = sdsnew((char *)c->argv[0]->ptr);
3771        sdstoupper(cmd);
3772        *err = sdsnew(NULL);
3773        *err = sdscatprintf(*err, "unknown subcommand '%.128s'. Try %s HELP.",
3774                            (char *)c->argv[1]->ptr, cmd);
3775        sdsfree(cmd);
3776    } else {
3777        sds args = sdsempty();
3778        int i;
3779        for (i=1; i < c->argc && sdslen(args) < 128; i++)
3780            args = sdscatprintf(args, "'%.*s' ", 128-(int)sdslen(args), (char*)c->argv[i]->ptr);
3781        *err = sdsnew(NULL);
3782        *err = sdscatprintf(*err, "unknown command '%.128s', with args beginning with: %s",
3783                            (char*)c->argv[0]->ptr, args);
3784        sdsfree(args);
3785    }
3786    /* Make sure there are no newlines in the string, otherwise invalid protocol
3787     * is emitted (The args come from the user, they may contain any character). */
3788    sdsmapchars(*err, "\r\n", "  ",  2);
3789    return 0;
3790}
3791
3792/* Check if c->argc is valid for c->cmd, fills `err` with details in case it isn't.
3793 * Return 1 if valid. */
3794int commandCheckArity(client *c, sds *err) {
3795    if ((c->cmd->arity > 0 && c->cmd->arity != c->argc) ||
3796        (c->argc < -c->cmd->arity))
3797    {
3798        if (err) {
3799            *err = sdsnew(NULL);
3800            *err = sdscatprintf(*err, "wrong number of arguments for '%s' command", c->cmd->fullname);
3801        }
3802        return 0;
3803    }
3804
3805    return 1;
3806}
3807
3808/* If we're executing a script, try to extract a set of command flags from
3809 * it, in case it declared them. Note this is just an attempt, we don't yet
3810 * know the script command is well formed.*/
3811uint64_t getCommandFlags(client *c) {
3812    uint64_t cmd_flags = c->cmd->flags;
3813
3814    if (c->cmd->proc == fcallCommand || c->cmd->proc == fcallroCommand) {
3815        cmd_flags = fcallGetCommandFlags(c, cmd_flags);
3816    } else if (c->cmd->proc == evalCommand || c->cmd->proc == evalRoCommand ||
3817               c->cmd->proc == evalShaCommand || c->cmd->proc == evalShaRoCommand)
3818    {
3819        cmd_flags = evalGetCommandFlags(c, cmd_flags);
3820    }
3821
3822    return cmd_flags;
3823}
3824
3825/* If this function gets called we already read a whole
3826 * command, arguments are in the client argv/argc fields.
3827 * processCommand() execute the command or prepare the
3828 * server for a bulk read from the client.
3829 *
3830 * If C_OK is returned the client is still alive and valid and
3831 * other operations can be performed by the caller. Otherwise
3832 * if C_ERR is returned the client was destroyed (i.e. after QUIT). */
3833int processCommand(client *c) {
3834    if (!scriptIsTimedout()) {
3835        /* Both EXEC and scripts call call() directly so there should be
3836         * no way in_exec or scriptIsRunning() is 1.
3837         * That is unless lua_timedout, in which case client may run
3838         * some commands. */
3839        serverAssert(!server.in_exec);
3840        serverAssert(!scriptIsRunning());
3841    }
3842
3843    /* in case we are starting to ProcessCommand and we already have a command we assume
3844     * this is a reprocessing of this command, so we do not want to perform some of the actions again. */
3845    int client_reprocessing_command = c->cmd ? 1 : 0;
3846
3847    /* only run command filter if not reprocessing command */
3848    if (!client_reprocessing_command) {
3849        moduleCallCommandFilters(c);
3850        reqresAppendRequest(c);
3851    }
3852
3853    /* Handle possible security attacks. */
3854    if (!strcasecmp(c->argv[0]->ptr,"host:") || !strcasecmp(c->argv[0]->ptr,"post")) {
3855        securityWarningCommand(c);
3856        return C_ERR;
3857    }
3858
3859    /* If we're inside a module blocked context yielding that wants to avoid
3860     * processing clients, postpone the command. */
3861    if (server.busy_module_yield_flags != BUSY_MODULE_YIELD_NONE &&
3862        !(server.busy_module_yield_flags & BUSY_MODULE_YIELD_CLIENTS))
3863    {
3864        blockPostponeClient(c);
3865        return C_OK;
3866    }
3867
3868    /* Now lookup the command and check ASAP about trivial error conditions
3869     * such as wrong arity, bad command name and so forth.
3870     * In case we are reprocessing a command after it was blocked,
3871     * we do not have to repeat the same checks */
3872    if (!client_reprocessing_command) {
3873        c->cmd = c->lastcmd = c->realcmd = lookupCommand(c->argv,c->argc);
3874        sds err;
3875        if (!commandCheckExistence(c, &err)) {
3876            rejectCommandSds(c, err);
3877            return C_OK;
3878        }
3879        if (!commandCheckArity(c, &err)) {
3880            rejectCommandSds(c, err);
3881            return C_OK;
3882        }
3883
3884
3885        /* Check if the command is marked as protected and the relevant configuration allows it */
3886        if (c->cmd->flags & CMD_PROTECTED) {
3887            if ((c->cmd->proc == debugCommand && !allowProtectedAction(server.enable_debug_cmd, c)) ||
3888                (c->cmd->proc == moduleCommand && !allowProtectedAction(server.enable_module_cmd, c)))
3889            {
3890                rejectCommandFormat(c,"%s command not allowed. If the %s option is set to \"local\", "
3891                                      "you can run it from a local connection, otherwise you need to set this option "
3892                                      "in the configuration file, and then restart the server.",
3893                                      c->cmd->proc == debugCommand ? "DEBUG" : "MODULE",
3894                                      c->cmd->proc == debugCommand ? "enable-debug-command" : "enable-module-command");
3895                return C_OK;
3896
3897            }
3898        }
3899    }
3900
3901    uint64_t cmd_flags = getCommandFlags(c);
3902
3903    int is_read_command = (cmd_flags & CMD_READONLY) ||
3904                           (c->cmd->proc == execCommand && (c->mstate.cmd_flags & CMD_READONLY));
3905    int is_write_command = (cmd_flags & CMD_WRITE) ||
3906                           (c->cmd->proc == execCommand && (c->mstate.cmd_flags & CMD_WRITE));
3907    int is_denyoom_command = (cmd_flags & CMD_DENYOOM) ||
3908                             (c->cmd->proc == execCommand && (c->mstate.cmd_flags & CMD_DENYOOM));
3909    int is_denystale_command = !(cmd_flags & CMD_STALE) ||
3910                               (c->cmd->proc == execCommand && (c->mstate.cmd_inv_flags & CMD_STALE));
3911    int is_denyloading_command = !(cmd_flags & CMD_LOADING) ||
3912                                 (c->cmd->proc == execCommand && (c->mstate.cmd_inv_flags & CMD_LOADING));
3913    int is_may_replicate_command = (cmd_flags & (CMD_WRITE | CMD_MAY_REPLICATE)) ||
3914                                   (c->cmd->proc == execCommand && (c->mstate.cmd_flags & (CMD_WRITE | CMD_MAY_REPLICATE)));
3915    int is_deny_async_loading_command = (cmd_flags & CMD_NO_ASYNC_LOADING) ||
3916                                        (c->cmd->proc == execCommand && (c->mstate.cmd_flags & CMD_NO_ASYNC_LOADING));
3917    int obey_client = mustObeyClient(c);
3918
3919    if (authRequired(c)) {
3920        /* AUTH and HELLO and no auth commands are valid even in
3921         * non-authenticated state. */
3922        if (!(c->cmd->flags & CMD_NO_AUTH)) {
3923            rejectCommand(c,shared.noautherr);
3924            return C_OK;
3925        }
3926    }
3927
3928    if (c->flags & CLIENT_MULTI && c->cmd->flags & CMD_NO_MULTI) {
3929        rejectCommandFormat(c,"Command not allowed inside a transaction");
3930        return C_OK;
3931    }
3932
3933    /* Check if the user can run this command according to the current
3934     * ACLs. */
3935    int acl_errpos;
3936    int acl_retval = ACLCheckAllPerm(c,&acl_errpos);
3937    if (acl_retval != ACL_OK) {
3938        addACLLogEntry(c,acl_retval,(c->flags & CLIENT_MULTI) ? ACL_LOG_CTX_MULTI : ACL_LOG_CTX_TOPLEVEL,acl_errpos,NULL,NULL);
3939        sds msg = getAclErrorMessage(acl_retval, c->user, c->cmd, c->argv[acl_errpos]->ptr, 0);
3940        rejectCommandFormat(c, "-NOPERM %s", msg);
3941        sdsfree(msg);
3942        return C_OK;
3943    }
3944
3945    /* If cluster is enabled perform the cluster redirection here.
3946     * However we don't perform the redirection if:
3947     * 1) The sender of this command is our master.
3948     * 2) The command has no key arguments. */
3949    if (server.cluster_enabled &&
3950        !mustObeyClient(c) &&
3951        !(!(c->cmd->flags&CMD_MOVABLE_KEYS) && c->cmd->key_specs_num == 0 &&
3952          c->cmd->proc != execCommand))
3953    {
3954        int error_code;
3955        clusterNode *n = getNodeByQuery(c,c->cmd,c->argv,c->argc,
3956                                        &c->slot,&error_code);
3957        if (n == NULL || n != server.cluster->myself) {
3958            if (c->cmd->proc == execCommand) {
3959                discardTransaction(c);
3960            } else {
3961                flagTransaction(c);
3962            }
3963            clusterRedirectClient(c,n,c->slot,error_code);
3964            c->cmd->rejected_calls++;
3965            return C_OK;
3966        }
3967    }
3968
3969    /* Disconnect some clients if total clients memory is too high. We do this
3970     * before key eviction, after the last command was executed and consumed
3971     * some client output buffer memory. */
3972    evictClients();
3973    if (server.current_client == NULL) {
3974        /* If we evicted ourself then abort processing the command */
3975        return C_ERR;
3976    }
3977
3978    /* Handle the maxmemory directive.
3979     *
3980     * Note that we do not want to reclaim memory if we are here re-entering
3981     * the event loop since there is a busy Lua script running in timeout
3982     * condition, to avoid mixing the propagation of scripts with the
3983     * propagation of DELs due to eviction. */
3984    if (server.maxmemory && !isInsideYieldingLongCommand()) {
3985        int out_of_memory = (performEvictions() == EVICT_FAIL);
3986
3987        /* performEvictions may evict keys, so we need flush pending tracking
3988         * invalidation keys. If we don't do this, we may get an invalidation
3989         * message after we perform operation on the key, where in fact this
3990         * message belongs to the old value of the key before it gets evicted.*/
3991        trackingHandlePendingKeyInvalidations();
3992
3993        /* performEvictions may flush slave output buffers. This may result
3994         * in a slave, that may be the active client, to be freed. */
3995        if (server.current_client == NULL) return C_ERR;
3996
3997        int reject_cmd_on_oom = is_denyoom_command;
3998        /* If client is in MULTI/EXEC context, queuing may consume an unlimited
3999         * amount of memory, so we want to stop that.
4000         * However, we never want to reject DISCARD, or even EXEC (unless it
4001         * contains denied commands, in which case is_denyoom_command is already
4002         * set. */
4003        if (c->flags & CLIENT_MULTI &&
4004            c->cmd->proc != execCommand &&
4005            c->cmd->proc != discardCommand &&
4006            c->cmd->proc != quitCommand &&
4007            c->cmd->proc != resetCommand) {
4008            reject_cmd_on_oom = 1;
4009        }
4010
4011        if (out_of_memory && reject_cmd_on_oom) {
4012            rejectCommand(c, shared.oomerr);
4013            return C_OK;
4014        }
4015
4016        /* Save out_of_memory result at command start, otherwise if we check OOM
4017         * in the first write within script, memory used by lua stack and
4018         * arguments might interfere. We need to save it for EXEC and module
4019         * calls too, since these can call EVAL, but avoid saving it during an
4020         * interrupted / yielding busy script / module. */
4021        server.pre_command_oom_state = out_of_memory;
4022    }
4023
4024    /* Make sure to use a reasonable amount of memory for client side
4025     * caching metadata. */
4026    if (server.tracking_clients) trackingLimitUsedSlots();
4027
4028    /* Don't accept write commands if there are problems persisting on disk
4029     * unless coming from our master, in which case check the replica ignore
4030     * disk write error config to either log or crash. */
4031    int deny_write_type = writeCommandsDeniedByDiskError();
4032    if (deny_write_type != DISK_ERROR_TYPE_NONE &&
4033        (is_write_command || c->cmd->proc == pingCommand))
4034    {
4035        if (obey_client) {
4036            if (!server.repl_ignore_disk_write_error && c->cmd->proc != pingCommand) {
4037                serverPanic("Replica was unable to write command to disk.");
4038            } else {
4039                static mstime_t last_log_time_ms = 0;
4040                const mstime_t log_interval_ms = 10000;
4041                if (server.mstime > last_log_time_ms + log_interval_ms) {
4042                    last_log_time_ms = server.mstime;
4043                    serverLog(LL_WARNING, "Replica is applying a command even though "
4044                                          "it is unable to write to disk.");
4045                }
4046            }
4047        } else {
4048            sds err = writeCommandsGetDiskErrorMessage(deny_write_type);
4049            /* remove the newline since rejectCommandSds adds it. */
4050            sdssubstr(err, 0, sdslen(err)-2);
4051            rejectCommandSds(c, err);
4052            return C_OK;
4053        }
4054    }
4055
4056    /* Don't accept write commands if there are not enough good slaves and
4057     * user configured the min-slaves-to-write option. */
4058    if (is_write_command && !checkGoodReplicasStatus()) {
4059        rejectCommand(c, shared.noreplicaserr);
4060        return C_OK;
4061    }
4062
4063    /* Don't accept write commands if this is a read only slave. But
4064     * accept write commands if this is our master. */
4065    if (server.masterhost && server.repl_slave_ro &&
4066        !obey_client &&
4067        is_write_command)
4068    {
4069        rejectCommand(c, shared.roslaveerr);
4070        return C_OK;
4071    }
4072
4073    /* Only allow a subset of commands in the context of Pub/Sub if the
4074     * connection is in RESP2 mode. With RESP3 there are no limits. */
4075    if ((c->flags & CLIENT_PUBSUB && c->resp == 2) &&
4076        c->cmd->proc != pingCommand &&
4077        c->cmd->proc != subscribeCommand &&
4078        c->cmd->proc != ssubscribeCommand &&
4079        c->cmd->proc != unsubscribeCommand &&
4080        c->cmd->proc != sunsubscribeCommand &&
4081        c->cmd->proc != psubscribeCommand &&
4082        c->cmd->proc != punsubscribeCommand &&
4083        c->cmd->proc != quitCommand &&
4084        c->cmd->proc != resetCommand) {
4085        rejectCommandFormat(c,
4086            "Can't execute '%s': only (P|S)SUBSCRIBE / "
4087            "(P|S)UNSUBSCRIBE / PING / QUIT / RESET are allowed in this context",
4088            c->cmd->fullname);
4089        return C_OK;
4090    }
4091
4092    /* Only allow commands with flag "t", such as INFO, REPLICAOF and so on,
4093     * when replica-serve-stale-data is no and we are a replica with a broken
4094     * link with master. */
4095    if (server.masterhost && server.repl_state != REPL_STATE_CONNECTED &&
4096        server.repl_serve_stale_data == 0 &&
4097        is_denystale_command)
4098    {
4099        rejectCommand(c, shared.masterdownerr);
4100        return C_OK;
4101    }
4102
4103    /* Loading DB? Return an error if the command has not the
4104     * CMD_LOADING flag. */
4105    if (server.loading && !server.async_loading && is_denyloading_command) {
4106        rejectCommand(c, shared.loadingerr);
4107        return C_OK;
4108    }
4109
4110    /* During async-loading, block certain commands. */
4111    if (server.async_loading && is_deny_async_loading_command) {
4112        rejectCommand(c,shared.loadingerr);
4113        return C_OK;
4114    }
4115
4116    /* when a busy job is being done (script / module)
4117     * Only allow a limited number of commands.
4118     * Note that we need to allow the transactions commands, otherwise clients
4119     * sending a transaction with pipelining without error checking, may have
4120     * the MULTI plus a few initial commands refused, then the timeout
4121     * condition resolves, and the bottom-half of the transaction gets
4122     * executed, see Github PR #7022. */
4123    if (isInsideYieldingLongCommand() && !(c->cmd->flags & CMD_ALLOW_BUSY)) {
4124        if (server.busy_module_yield_flags && server.busy_module_yield_reply) {
4125            rejectCommandFormat(c, "-BUSY %s", server.busy_module_yield_reply);
4126        } else if (server.busy_module_yield_flags) {
4127            rejectCommand(c, shared.slowmoduleerr);
4128        } else if (scriptIsEval()) {
4129            rejectCommand(c, shared.slowevalerr);
4130        } else {
4131            rejectCommand(c, shared.slowscripterr);
4132        }
4133        return C_OK;
4134    }
4135
4136    /* Prevent a replica from sending commands that access the keyspace.
4137     * The main objective here is to prevent abuse of client pause check
4138     * from which replicas are exempt. */
4139    if ((c->flags & CLIENT_SLAVE) && (is_may_replicate_command || is_write_command || is_read_command)) {
4140        rejectCommandFormat(c, "Replica can't interact with the keyspace");
4141        return C_OK;
4142    }
4143
4144    /* If the server is paused, block the client until
4145     * the pause has ended. Replicas are never paused. */
4146    if (!(c->flags & CLIENT_SLAVE) && 
4147        ((isPausedActions(PAUSE_ACTION_CLIENT_ALL)) ||
4148        ((isPausedActions(PAUSE_ACTION_CLIENT_WRITE)) && is_may_replicate_command)))
4149    {
4150        blockPostponeClient(c);
4151        return C_OK;       
4152    }
4153
4154    /* Exec the command */
4155    if (c->flags & CLIENT_MULTI &&
4156        c->cmd->proc != execCommand &&
4157        c->cmd->proc != discardCommand &&
4158        c->cmd->proc != multiCommand &&
4159        c->cmd->proc != watchCommand &&
4160        c->cmd->proc != quitCommand &&
4161        c->cmd->proc != resetCommand)
4162    {
4163        queueMultiCommand(c, cmd_flags);
4164        addReply(c,shared.queued);
4165    } else {
4166        int flags = CMD_CALL_FULL;
4167        if (client_reprocessing_command) flags |= CMD_CALL_REPROCESSING;
4168        call(c,flags);
4169        if (listLength(server.ready_keys) && !isInsideYieldingLongCommand())
4170            handleClientsBlockedOnKeys();
4171    }
4172
4173    return C_OK;
4174}
4175
4176/* ====================== Error lookup and execution ===================== */
4177
4178void incrementErrorCount(const char *fullerr, size_t namelen) {
4179    struct redisError *error = raxFind(server.errors,(unsigned char*)fullerr,namelen);
4180    if (error == raxNotFound) {
4181        error = zmalloc(sizeof(*error));
4182        error->count = 0;
4183        raxInsert(server.errors,(unsigned char*)fullerr,namelen,error,NULL);
4184    }
4185    error->count++;
4186}
4187
4188/*================================== Shutdown =============================== */
4189
4190/* Close listening sockets. Also unlink the unix domain socket if
4191 * unlink_unix_socket is non-zero. */
4192void closeListeningSockets(int unlink_unix_socket) {
4193    int j;
4194
4195    for (int i = 0; i < CONN_TYPE_MAX; i++) {
4196        connListener *listener = &server.listeners[i];
4197        if (listener->ct == NULL)
4198            continue;
4199
4200        for (j = 0; j < listener->count; j++) close(listener->fd[j]);
4201    }
4202
4203    if (server.cluster_enabled)
4204        for (j = 0; j < server.clistener.count; j++) close(server.clistener.fd[j]);
4205    if (unlink_unix_socket && server.unixsocket) {
4206        serverLog(LL_NOTICE,"Removing the unix socket file.");
4207        if (unlink(server.unixsocket) != 0)
4208            serverLog(LL_WARNING,"Error removing the unix socket file: %s",strerror(errno));
4209    }
4210}
4211
4212/* Prepare for shutting down the server. Flags:
4213 *
4214 * - SHUTDOWN_SAVE: Save a database dump even if the server is configured not to
4215 *   save any dump.
4216 *
4217 * - SHUTDOWN_NOSAVE: Don't save any database dump even if the server is
4218 *   configured to save one.
4219 *
4220 * - SHUTDOWN_NOW: Don't wait for replicas to catch up before shutting down.
4221 *
4222 * - SHUTDOWN_FORCE: Ignore errors writing AOF and RDB files on disk, which
4223 *   would normally prevent a shutdown.
4224 *
4225 * Unless SHUTDOWN_NOW is set and if any replicas are lagging behind, C_ERR is
4226 * returned and server.shutdown_mstime is set to a timestamp to allow a grace
4227 * period for the replicas to catch up. This is checked and handled by
4228 * serverCron() which completes the shutdown as soon as possible.
4229 *
4230 * If shutting down fails due to errors writing RDB or AOF files, C_ERR is
4231 * returned and an error is logged. If the flag SHUTDOWN_FORCE is set, these
4232 * errors are logged but ignored and C_OK is returned.
4233 *
4234 * On success, this function returns C_OK and then it's OK to call exit(0). */
4235int prepareForShutdown(int flags) {
4236    if (isShutdownInitiated()) return C_ERR;
4237
4238    /* When SHUTDOWN is called while the server is loading a dataset in
4239     * memory we need to make sure no attempt is performed to save
4240     * the dataset on shutdown (otherwise it could overwrite the current DB
4241     * with half-read data).
4242     *
4243     * Also when in Sentinel mode clear the SAVE flag and force NOSAVE. */
4244    if (server.loading || server.sentinel_mode)
4245        flags = (flags & ~SHUTDOWN_SAVE) | SHUTDOWN_NOSAVE;
4246
4247    server.shutdown_flags = flags;
4248
4249    serverLog(LL_NOTICE,"User requested shutdown...");
4250    if (server.supervised_mode == SUPERVISED_SYSTEMD)
4251        redisCommunicateSystemd("STOPPING=1\n");
4252
4253    /* If we have any replicas, let them catch up the replication offset before
4254     * we shut down, to avoid data loss. */
4255    if (!(flags & SHUTDOWN_NOW) &&
4256        server.shutdown_timeout != 0 &&
4257        !isReadyToShutdown())
4258    {
4259        server.shutdown_mstime = server.mstime + server.shutdown_timeout * 1000;
4260        if (!isPausedActions(PAUSE_ACTION_REPLICA)) sendGetackToReplicas();
4261        pauseActions(PAUSE_DURING_SHUTDOWN,
4262                      LLONG_MAX,
4263                     PAUSE_ACTIONS_CLIENT_WRITE_SET);
4264        serverLog(LL_NOTICE, "Waiting for replicas before shutting down.");
4265        return C_ERR;
4266    }
4267
4268    return finishShutdown();
4269}
4270
4271static inline int isShutdownInitiated(void) {
4272    return server.shutdown_mstime != 0;
4273}
4274
4275/* Returns 0 if there are any replicas which are lagging in replication which we
4276 * need to wait for before shutting down. Returns 1 if we're ready to shut
4277 * down now. */
4278int isReadyToShutdown(void) {
4279    if (listLength(server.slaves) == 0) return 1;  /* No replicas. */
4280
4281    listIter li;
4282    listNode *ln;
4283    listRewind(server.slaves, &li);
4284    while ((ln = listNext(&li)) != NULL) {
4285        client *replica = listNodeValue(ln);
4286        if (replica->repl_ack_off != server.master_repl_offset) return 0;
4287    }
4288    return 1;
4289}
4290
4291static void cancelShutdown(void) {
4292    server.shutdown_asap = 0;
4293    server.shutdown_flags = 0;
4294    server.shutdown_mstime = 0;
4295    server.last_sig_received = 0;
4296    replyToClientsBlockedOnShutdown();
4297    unpauseActions(PAUSE_DURING_SHUTDOWN);
4298}
4299
4300/* Returns C_OK if shutdown was aborted and C_ERR if shutdown wasn't ongoing. */
4301int abortShutdown(void) {
4302    if (isShutdownInitiated()) {
4303        cancelShutdown();
4304    } else if (server.shutdown_asap) {
4305        /* Signal handler has requested shutdown, but it hasn't been initiated
4306         * yet. Just clear the flag. */
4307        server.shutdown_asap = 0;
4308    } else {
4309        /* Shutdown neither initiated nor requested. */
4310        return C_ERR;
4311    }
4312    serverLog(LL_NOTICE, "Shutdown manually aborted.");
4313    return C_OK;
4314}
4315
4316/* The final step of the shutdown sequence. Returns C_OK if the shutdown
4317 * sequence was successful and it's OK to call exit(). If C_ERR is returned,
4318 * it's not safe to call exit(). */
4319int finishShutdown(void) {
4320
4321    int save = server.shutdown_flags & SHUTDOWN_SAVE;
4322    int nosave = server.shutdown_flags & SHUTDOWN_NOSAVE;
4323    int force = server.shutdown_flags & SHUTDOWN_FORCE;
4324
4325    /* Log a warning for each replica that is lagging. */
4326    listIter replicas_iter;
4327    listNode *replicas_list_node;
4328    int num_replicas = 0, num_lagging_replicas = 0;
4329    listRewind(server.slaves, &replicas_iter);
4330    while ((replicas_list_node = listNext(&replicas_iter)) != NULL) {
4331        client *replica = listNodeValue(replicas_list_node);
4332        num_replicas++;
4333        if (replica->repl_ack_off != server.master_repl_offset) {
4334            num_lagging_replicas++;
4335            long lag = replica->replstate == SLAVE_STATE_ONLINE ?
4336                time(NULL) - replica->repl_ack_time : 0;
4337            serverLog(LL_NOTICE,
4338                      "Lagging replica %s reported offset %lld behind master, lag=%ld, state=%s.",
4339                      replicationGetSlaveName(replica),
4340                      server.master_repl_offset - replica->repl_ack_off,
4341                      lag,
4342                      replstateToString(replica->replstate));
4343        }
4344    }
4345    if (num_replicas > 0) {
4346        serverLog(LL_NOTICE,
4347                  "%d of %d replicas are in sync when shutting down.",
4348                  num_replicas - num_lagging_replicas,
4349                  num_replicas);
4350    }
4351
4352    /* Kill all the Lua debugger forked sessions. */
4353    ldbKillForkedSessions();
4354
4355    /* Kill the saving child if there is a background saving in progress.
4356       We want to avoid race conditions, for instance our saving child may
4357       overwrite the synchronous saving did by SHUTDOWN. */
4358    if (server.child_type == CHILD_TYPE_RDB) {
4359        serverLog(LL_WARNING,"There is a child saving an .rdb. Killing it!");
4360        killRDBChild();
4361        /* Note that, in killRDBChild normally has backgroundSaveDoneHandler
4362         * doing it's cleanup, but in this case this code will not be reached,
4363         * so we need to call rdbRemoveTempFile which will close fd(in order
4364         * to unlink file actually) in background thread.
4365         * The temp rdb file fd may won't be closed when redis exits quickly,
4366         * but OS will close this fd when process exits. */
4367        rdbRemoveTempFile(server.child_pid, 0);
4368    }
4369
4370    /* Kill module child if there is one. */
4371    if (server.child_type == CHILD_TYPE_MODULE) {
4372        serverLog(LL_WARNING,"There is a module fork child. Killing it!");
4373        TerminateModuleForkChild(server.child_pid,0);
4374    }
4375
4376    /* Kill the AOF saving child as the AOF we already have may be longer
4377     * but contains the full dataset anyway. */
4378    if (server.child_type == CHILD_TYPE_AOF) {
4379        /* If we have AOF enabled but haven't written the AOF yet, don't
4380         * shutdown or else the dataset will be lost. */
4381        if (server.aof_state == AOF_WAIT_REWRITE) {
4382            if (force) {
4383                serverLog(LL_WARNING, "Writing initial AOF. Exit anyway.");
4384            } else {
4385                serverLog(LL_WARNING, "Writing initial AOF, can't exit.");
4386                if (server.supervised_mode == SUPERVISED_SYSTEMD)
4387                    redisCommunicateSystemd("STATUS=Writing initial AOF, can't exit.\n");
4388                goto error;
4389            }
4390        }
4391        serverLog(LL_WARNING,
4392                  "There is a child rewriting the AOF. Killing it!");
4393        killAppendOnlyChild();
4394    }
4395    if (server.aof_state != AOF_OFF) {
4396        /* Append only file: flush buffers and fsync() the AOF at exit */
4397        serverLog(LL_NOTICE,"Calling fsync() on the AOF file.");
4398        flushAppendOnlyFile(1);
4399        if (redis_fsync(server.aof_fd) == -1) {
4400            serverLog(LL_WARNING,"Fail to fsync the AOF file: %s.",
4401                                 strerror(errno));
4402        }
4403    }
4404
4405    /* Create a new RDB file before exiting. */
4406    if ((server.saveparamslen > 0 && !nosave) || save) {
4407        serverLog(LL_NOTICE,"Saving the final RDB snapshot before exiting.");
4408        if (server.supervised_mode == SUPERVISED_SYSTEMD)
4409            redisCommunicateSystemd("STATUS=Saving the final RDB snapshot\n");
4410        /* Snapshotting. Perform a SYNC SAVE and exit */
4411        rdbSaveInfo rsi, *rsiptr;
4412        rsiptr = rdbPopulateSaveInfo(&rsi);
4413        /* Keep the page cache since it's likely to restart soon */
4414        if (rdbSave(SLAVE_REQ_NONE,server.rdb_filename,rsiptr,RDBFLAGS_KEEP_CACHE) != C_OK) {
4415            /* Ooops.. error saving! The best we can do is to continue
4416             * operating. Note that if there was a background saving process,
4417             * in the next cron() Redis will be notified that the background
4418             * saving aborted, handling special stuff like slaves pending for
4419             * synchronization... */
4420            if (force) {
4421                serverLog(LL_WARNING,"Error trying to save the DB. Exit anyway.");
4422            } else {
4423                serverLog(LL_WARNING,"Error trying to save the DB, can't exit.");
4424                if (server.supervised_mode == SUPERVISED_SYSTEMD)
4425                    redisCommunicateSystemd("STATUS=Error trying to save the DB, can't exit.\n");
4426                goto error;
4427            }
4428        }
4429    }
4430
4431    /* Free the AOF manifest. */
4432    if (server.aof_manifest) aofManifestFree(server.aof_manifest);
4433
4434    /* Fire the shutdown modules event. */
4435    moduleFireServerEvent(REDISMODULE_EVENT_SHUTDOWN,0,NULL);
4436
4437    /* Remove the pid file if possible and needed. */
4438    if (server.daemonize || server.pidfile) {
4439        serverLog(LL_NOTICE,"Removing the pid file.");
4440        unlink(server.pidfile);
4441    }
4442
4443    /* Best effort flush of slave output buffers, so that we hopefully
4444     * send them pending writes. */
4445    flushSlavesOutputBuffers();
4446
4447    /* Close the listening sockets. Apparently this allows faster restarts. */
4448    closeListeningSockets(1);
4449
4450#if !defined(__sun)
4451    /* Unlock the cluster config file before shutdown */
4452    if (server.cluster_enabled && server.cluster_config_file_lock_fd != -1) {
4453        flock(server.cluster_config_file_lock_fd, LOCK_UN|LOCK_NB);
4454    }
4455#endif /* __sun */
4456
4457
4458    serverLog(LL_WARNING,"%s is now ready to exit, bye bye...",
4459        server.sentinel_mode ? "Sentinel" : "Redis");
4460    return C_OK;
4461
4462error:
4463    serverLog(LL_WARNING, "Errors trying to shut down the server. Check the logs for more information.");
4464    cancelShutdown();
4465    return C_ERR;
4466}
4467
4468/*================================== Commands =============================== */
4469
4470/* Sometimes Redis cannot accept write commands because there is a persistence
4471 * error with the RDB or AOF file, and Redis is configured in order to stop
4472 * accepting writes in such situation. This function returns if such a
4473 * condition is active, and the type of the condition.
4474 *
4475 * Function return values:
4476 *
4477 * DISK_ERROR_TYPE_NONE:    No problems, we can accept writes.
4478 * DISK_ERROR_TYPE_AOF:     Don't accept writes: AOF errors.
4479 * DISK_ERROR_TYPE_RDB:     Don't accept writes: RDB errors.
4480 */
4481int writeCommandsDeniedByDiskError(void) {
4482    if (server.stop_writes_on_bgsave_err &&
4483        server.saveparamslen > 0 &&
4484        server.lastbgsave_status == C_ERR)
4485    {
4486        return DISK_ERROR_TYPE_RDB;
4487    } else if (server.aof_state != AOF_OFF) {
4488        if (server.aof_last_write_status == C_ERR) {
4489            return DISK_ERROR_TYPE_AOF;
4490        }
4491        /* AOF fsync error. */
4492        int aof_bio_fsync_status;
4493        atomicGet(server.aof_bio_fsync_status,aof_bio_fsync_status);
4494        if (aof_bio_fsync_status == C_ERR) {
4495            atomicGet(server.aof_bio_fsync_errno,server.aof_last_write_errno);
4496            return DISK_ERROR_TYPE_AOF;
4497        }
4498    }
4499
4500    return DISK_ERROR_TYPE_NONE;
4501}
4502
4503sds writeCommandsGetDiskErrorMessage(int error_code) {
4504    sds ret = NULL;
4505    if (error_code == DISK_ERROR_TYPE_RDB) {
4506        ret = sdsdup(shared.bgsaveerr->ptr);
4507    } else {
4508        ret = sdscatfmt(sdsempty(),
4509                "-MISCONF Errors writing to the AOF file: %s\r\n",
4510                strerror(server.aof_last_write_errno));
4511    }
4512    return ret;
4513}
4514
4515/* The PING command. It works in a different way if the client is in
4516 * in Pub/Sub mode. */
4517void pingCommand(client *c) {
4518    /* The command takes zero or one arguments. */
4519    if (c->argc > 2) {
4520        addReplyErrorArity(c);
4521        return;
4522    }
4523
4524    if (c->flags & CLIENT_PUBSUB && c->resp == 2) {
4525        addReply(c,shared.mbulkhdr[2]);
4526        addReplyBulkCBuffer(c,"pong",4);
4527        if (c->argc == 1)
4528            addReplyBulkCBuffer(c,"",0);
4529        else
4530            addReplyBulk(c,c->argv[1]);
4531    } else {
4532        if (c->argc == 1)
4533            addReply(c,shared.pong);
4534        else
4535            addReplyBulk(c,c->argv[1]);
4536    }
4537}
4538
4539void echoCommand(client *c) {
4540    addReplyBulk(c,c->argv[1]);
4541}
4542
4543void timeCommand(client *c) {
4544    addReplyArrayLen(c,2);
4545    addReplyBulkLongLong(c, server.unixtime);
4546    addReplyBulkLongLong(c, server.ustime-((long long)server.unixtime)*1000000);
4547}
4548
4549typedef struct replyFlagNames {
4550    uint64_t flag;
4551    const char *name;
4552} replyFlagNames;
4553
4554/* Helper function to output flags. */
4555void addReplyCommandFlags(client *c, uint64_t flags, replyFlagNames *replyFlags) {
4556    int count = 0, j=0;
4557    /* Count them so we don't have to use deferred reply. */
4558    while (replyFlags[j].name) {
4559        if (flags & replyFlags[j].flag)
4560            count++;
4561        j++;
4562    }
4563
4564    addReplySetLen(c, count);
4565    j = 0;
4566    while (replyFlags[j].name) {
4567        if (flags & replyFlags[j].flag)
4568            addReplyStatus(c, replyFlags[j].name);
4569        j++;
4570    }
4571}
4572
4573void addReplyFlagsForCommand(client *c, struct redisCommand *cmd) {
4574    replyFlagNames flagNames[] = {
4575        {CMD_WRITE,             "write"},
4576        {CMD_READONLY,          "readonly"},
4577        {CMD_DENYOOM,           "denyoom"},
4578        {CMD_MODULE,            "module"},
4579        {CMD_ADMIN,             "admin"},
4580        {CMD_PUBSUB,            "pubsub"},
4581        {CMD_NOSCRIPT,          "noscript"},
4582        {CMD_BLOCKING,          "blocking"},
4583        {CMD_LOADING,           "loading"},
4584        {CMD_STALE,             "stale"},
4585        {CMD_SKIP_MONITOR,      "skip_monitor"},
4586        {CMD_SKIP_SLOWLOG,      "skip_slowlog"},
4587        {CMD_ASKING,            "asking"},
4588        {CMD_FAST,              "fast"},
4589        {CMD_NO_AUTH,           "no_auth"},
4590        /* {CMD_MAY_REPLICATE,     "may_replicate"},, Hidden on purpose */
4591        /* {CMD_SENTINEL,          "sentinel"}, Hidden on purpose */
4592        /* {CMD_ONLY_SENTINEL,     "only_sentinel"}, Hidden on purpose */
4593        {CMD_NO_MANDATORY_KEYS, "no_mandatory_keys"},
4594        /* {CMD_PROTECTED,         "protected"}, Hidden on purpose */
4595        {CMD_NO_ASYNC_LOADING,  "no_async_loading"},
4596        {CMD_NO_MULTI,          "no_multi"},
4597        {CMD_MOVABLE_KEYS,      "movablekeys"},
4598        {CMD_ALLOW_BUSY,        "allow_busy"},
4599        /* {CMD_TOUCHES_ARBITRARY_KEYS,  "TOUCHES_ARBITRARY_KEYS"}, Hidden on purpose */
4600        {0,NULL}
4601    };
4602    addReplyCommandFlags(c, cmd->flags, flagNames);
4603}

4632/* Must match redisCommandArgType */
4633const char *ARG_TYPE_STR[] = {
4634    "string",
4635    "integer",
4636    "double",
4637    "key",
4638    "pattern",
4639    "unix-time",
4640    "pure-token",
4641    "oneof",
4642    "block",
4643};
4644
4645void addReplyFlagsForArg(client *c, uint64_t flags) {
4646    replyFlagNames argFlagNames[] = {
4647        {CMD_ARG_OPTIONAL,          "optional"},
4648        {CMD_ARG_MULTIPLE,          "multiple"},
4649        {CMD_ARG_MULTIPLE_TOKEN,    "multiple_token"},
4650        {0,NULL}
4651    };
4652    addReplyCommandFlags(c, flags, argFlagNames);
4653}
4654
4655void addReplyCommandArgList(client *c, struct redisCommandArg *args, int num_args) {
4656    addReplyArrayLen(c, num_args);
4657    for (int j = 0; j<num_args; j++) {
4658        /* Count our reply len so we don't have to use deferred reply. */
4659        int has_display_text = 1;
4660        long maplen = 2;
4661        if (args[j].key_spec_index != -1) maplen++;
4662        if (args[j].token) maplen++;
4663        if (args[j].summary) maplen++;
4664        if (args[j].since) maplen++;
4665        if (args[j].deprecated_since) maplen++;
4666        if (args[j].flags) maplen++;
4667        if (args[j].type == ARG_TYPE_ONEOF || args[j].type == ARG_TYPE_BLOCK) {
4668            has_display_text = 0;
4669            maplen++;
4670        }
4671        if (has_display_text) maplen++;
4672        addReplyMapLen(c, maplen);
4673
4674        addReplyBulkCString(c, "name");
4675        addReplyBulkCString(c, args[j].name);
4676
4677        addReplyBulkCString(c, "type");
4678        addReplyBulkCString(c, ARG_TYPE_STR[args[j].type]);
4679
4680        if (has_display_text) {
4681            addReplyBulkCString(c, "display_text");
4682            addReplyBulkCString(c, args[j].display_text ? args[j].display_text : args[j].name);
4683        }
4684        if (args[j].key_spec_index != -1) {
4685            addReplyBulkCString(c, "key_spec_index");
4686            addReplyLongLong(c, args[j].key_spec_index);
4687        }
4688        if (args[j].token) {
4689            addReplyBulkCString(c, "token");
4690            addReplyBulkCString(c, args[j].token);
4691        }
4692        if (args[j].summary) {
4693            addReplyBulkCString(c, "summary");
4694            addReplyBulkCString(c, args[j].summary);
4695        }
4696        if (args[j].since) {
4697            addReplyBulkCString(c, "since");
4698            addReplyBulkCString(c, args[j].since);
4699        }
4700        if (args[j].deprecated_since) {
4701            addReplyBulkCString(c, "deprecated_since");
4702            addReplyBulkCString(c, args[j].deprecated_since);
4703        }
4704        if (args[j].flags) {
4705            addReplyBulkCString(c, "flags");
4706            addReplyFlagsForArg(c, args[j].flags);
4707        }
4708        if (args[j].type == ARG_TYPE_ONEOF || args[j].type == ARG_TYPE_BLOCK) {
4709            addReplyBulkCString(c, "arguments");
4710            addReplyCommandArgList(c, args[j].subargs, args[j].num_args);
4711        }
4712    }
4713}
4714
4715#ifdef LOG_REQ_RES
4716
4717void addReplyJson(client *c, struct jsonObject *rs) {
4718    addReplyMapLen(c, rs->length);
4719
4720    for (int i = 0; i < rs->length; i++) {
4721        struct jsonObjectElement *curr = &rs->elements[i];
4722        addReplyBulkCString(c, curr->key);
4723        switch (curr->type) {
4724        case (JSON_TYPE_BOOLEAN):
4725            addReplyBool(c, curr->value.boolean);
4726            break;
4727        case (JSON_TYPE_INTEGER):
4728            addReplyLongLong(c, curr->value.integer);
4729            break;
4730        case (JSON_TYPE_STRING):
4731            addReplyBulkCString(c, curr->value.string);
4732            break;
4733        case (JSON_TYPE_OBJECT):
4734            addReplyJson(c, curr->value.object);
4735            break;
4736        case (JSON_TYPE_ARRAY):
4737            addReplyArrayLen(c, curr->value.array.length);
4738            for (int k = 0; k < curr->value.array.length; k++) {
4739                struct jsonObject *object = curr->value.array.objects[k];
4740                addReplyJson(c, object);
4741            }
4742            break;
4743        default:
4744            serverPanic("Invalid JSON type %d", curr->type);
4745        }
4746    }
4747}
4748
4749#endif

4751void addReplyCommandHistory(client *c, struct redisCommand *cmd) {
4752    addReplySetLen(c, cmd->num_history);
4753    for (int j = 0; j<cmd->num_history; j++) {
4754        addReplyArrayLen(c, 2);
4755        addReplyBulkCString(c, cmd->history[j].since);
4756        addReplyBulkCString(c, cmd->history[j].changes);
4757    }
4758}
